id: "72818593"
title: "Foreign involvement in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)"
---

# Foreign involvement in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)

On 24 February 2022, Russia launched a [full-scale invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), escalating the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md) that had begun in 2014 and marking the largest military conflict in Europe since World War II. As of 30 June 2025, Ukraine had received approximately €64.6 billion (US \$75 billion) in military aid from the United States and about €84.7 billion (US \$99 billion) from other international allies, primarily through drawdowns of existing stockpiles that were then delivered to Ukrainian forces.[^cite_note-Kiel2025-1] As existing stockpiles are expended, the allied industrial base has been gradually drawn in to supply Ukraine. Since January 2022 and as of August 2025, mostly Western nations have pledged at least €309 billion (US \$360 billion) in aid to Ukraine, including approximately €149.3 billion (US \$174 billion) in direct military assistance from individual countries.[^cite_note-AlJazeera2025-2]

Additional countries have also contributed, with Canada pledging CA\$22 billion in assistance including CA\$1.46 billion in military aid,[^cite_note-Canada2025-3] Japan committing ¥1.5 trillion in loans and grants,[^cite_note-Japan2025-4] Australia providing A\$2 billion in support,[^cite_note-Australia2025-5] South Korea pledging US\$394 million for 2024 and an additional US\$100 million in April 2025,[^cite_note-SKorea2023-6][^cite_note-SKorea2025-7] and Turkey supplying Bayraktar TB2 drones through donations and co-production agreements.[^cite_note-Turkey2022-8]

By the beginning of 2025, the United States has provided around half of all military aid to Ukraine, with European allies providing the other half.[^cite_note-\:6-9] According to defense expert Malcolm Chalmers, at the beginning of 2025 US provided 20% of all military equipment Ukraine was using, with 25% provided by Europe and 55% produced by Ukraine. However, the 20% supplied by the US "is the most lethal and important."[^cite_note-\:2-10]

Since 2022, no major state actor has matched the West in overt military assistance to Moscow; instead, Russia’s most significant external support has been economic. China accounted for roughly 35 percent of Russia’s oil and gas export revenue in 2024—about US \$83 billion of the \$241 billion total—providing a critical balance-of-payments lifeline despite Western sanctions.[^cite_note-USCC2025-11] Iran has also supplied hundreds of Shahed loitering munitions to Russian forces, augmenting Moscow’s capabilities on the battlefield.[^cite_note-ReutersShahed-12] In addition to this, Iran has trained thousands of Russian personnel to operate the drones.[^cite_note-13]

## Foreign military sales and aid to Ukraine

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of military aid to Ukraine during the Russo-Ukrainian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War).

Although Ukraine is not a member of the [North Atlantic Treaty Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty_Organization) (NATO) and does not have a military alliance with the United States or any NATO nation,[^cite_note-Evans_46-14] from 24 January to 3 August 2022, the [Kiel Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiel_Institute) has tracked \$84.2 billion from the 40 NATO-member countries in financial, humanitarian, and military aid to Ukraine.[^cite_note-auto9-15]

The US has provided the most military assistance, supplying \$29.3 billion from February 2022 to 3 February 2023,[^cite_note-ukrFactSheet3feb2023-16] with a cumulative \$41 billion to Ukraine as of 24 July 2023.[^cite_note-43rdDrawdown-17][^cite_note-acctgErrFY22FY23-18] To support Ukraine, many NATO members and allies, such as Germany and Sweden, have reversed past policies against providing offensive military aid. [European Union](9317.md) (EU) nations as a bloc have provided €3.1 billion in military support, including lethal arms, to Ukraine.[^cite_note-\:3-19][^cite_note-23billionInPledges-21] Bulgaria, a major manufacturer of Soviet-pattern weapons, has supplied Ukraine with more than €2 billion worth of arms and ammunition, including a third of the ammunition the Ukrainian military needed in the critical, early phase of the invasion; Bulgaria also provides fuel supplies, and at times has covered 40% of the Ukrainian armed forces' fuel needs.[^cite_note-22]

Between 2014 and 2021, the UK, US, EU, and NATO provided mostly non-lethal military aid to Ukraine.[^cite_note-RB-23] Lethal aid to the country increased in 2018, when the US began to sell it weapons, including [Javelin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FGM-148_Javelin) anti-tank missiles,[^cite_note-RB-23] and Ukraine agreed to purchase [TB2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayraktar_TB2) combat drones from Turkey in 2019.[^cite_note-24]

In 2022, Russia massed equipment and troops on Ukraine's borders. In response, the US worked with other NATO member states to transfer US-produced weapons to Ukraine.[^cite_note-\:1-25] The UK began to supply Ukraine with [NLAW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NLAW) and Javelin anti-tank weapons.[^cite_note-26] After the invasion, NATO member states including Germany agreed to supply weapons but NATO itself did not.[^cite_note-Al-Jazeera-2022-02-28-27][^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29] NATO and its members also refused to send troops into Ukraine or to establish a no fly-zone in case this led to a larger-scale war.[^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31]

In 2022, Congress approved more than \$112 billion in aid to Ukraine. In October 2023, the [Biden administration](65732174.md) requested \$61.4 billion more for Ukraine for the year ahead.[^cite_note-32] On 20 April 2024, the U.S. House of Representatives approved a \$95 billion aid package to Ukraine, Israel and Taiwan.[^cite_note-33]

In April 2024, the Latvian government approved a new aid package for Ukraine in 2024, totaling EUR 9.6 million. The package consists of EUR 5.3 million allocated for [Ukraine](31750.md)'s reconstruction and an additional EUR 4.3 million designated to support the Ukrainian Armed Forces through the [European Peace Facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Peace_Facility).[^cite_note-34]

### Aid via drawdown from existing stocks



Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) *(second from right)* at the [NATO Summit in Vilnius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Vilnius_summit) on 12 July 2023

On 26 February 2022, US Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) announced \$350 million in lethal military assistance, including anti-armor and anti-aircraft systems.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36] The next day, the EU stated it would purchase €450 million (US\$502 million) in lethal assistance and an additional €50 million (\$56 million) in non-lethal supplies for Ukraine with Poland handling distribution.[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] During the first week of the invasion, NATO member states supplied more than 17,000 anti-tank weapons to Ukraine;[^cite_note-39] by mid-March, the number was estimated to be more than 20,000.[^cite_note-40] In three tranches that were agreed in February, March and April 2022, the EU committed to €1.5 billion to support the capabilities and resilience of the Ukrainian Armed Forces and the protection of the Ukrainian civilian population under the purview of the [European Peace Facility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Peace_Facility) line.[^cite_note-41]

As of 11 April 2022, the US and its allies had provided Ukraine with approximately 25,000 anti-aircraft and 60,000 anti-tank weapon systems.[^cite_note-42] The following day, Russia received anti-tank missiles and RPGs from Iran, which were supplied through undercover networks via Iraq.[^cite_note-43] On 19 April 2022, Romania announced a planned reform to the government decree that regulates the export of military weapons and national defense products to provide these weapons to Ukraine as well as NATO allies.[^cite_note-plg-44] The [Romanian Ministry of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_National_Defence_\(Romania\)) developed the draft decree stating the decision was made because of Russia's aggression against Ukraine.[^cite_note-milinua-45] On 27 April, Defense Minister [Vasile Dincu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasile_Dincu) said his plan had been discontinued.[^cite_note-aapp-46] On 26 April 2022, the US convened a [conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Ramstein_Air_Base_meeting) in which representatives of more than 40 countries[^cite_note-UkraineContactGroup-50] met at the [Ramstein Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramstein_Air_Base) to discuss military support for Ukraine.[^cite_note-ukdg-49] On 28 April 2022, US President [Joe Biden](145422.md) asked [Congress](31756.md) for an additional \$33 billion to assist Ukraine, including \$20 billion to provide it with weapons.[^cite_note-51] On 5 May, Ukraine's Prime Minister [Denys Shmyhal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Shmyhal) announced Ukraine had received more than \$12 billion worth of weapons and financial aid from Western countries since the start of Russia's invasion on 24 February.[^cite_note-52] On 10 May, the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) passed legislation that would provide \$40 billion in new aid to Ukraine.[^cite_note-53] After the [US Senate](24909346.md) approved the legislation, Biden signed it into law on 21 May.[^cite_note-54][^cite_note-55]

On 23 May 2022, US Secretary of Defense Lloyd Austin thanked Denmark for offering [Harpoon missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harpoon_\(missile\)) and launcher to Ukraine.[^cite_note-harpoon1945-56] By 15 June 2023 additional Harpoon missiles were en route to Ukraine.[^cite_note-harpoonUdcg-57]

On 30 May 2022, French Foreign Minister [Catherine Colonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_Colonna) announced the supply of additional [CAESAR self-propelled howitzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CAESAR_self-propelled_howitzer) systems[^cite_note-milincaesar-58] that were mounted on the Renault [Sherpa 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renault_Sherpa_5) [6×6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6%C3%976) chassis. On 25 May, [Commander-in-Chief of the Armed Forces of Ukraine](63525946.md) [Valerii Zaluzhnyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valerii_Zaluzhnyi) said that the first batch was already on the front lines fighting the invader.[^cite_note-milin55th-59] On 10 June, the AFU demonstrated the combat systems to representatives from the press; by that date the Ukrainian gunners possessed 18 CAESAR units.[^cite_note-nrfr24-60][^cite_note-rfecae-61] On 31 May 2022, the [White House](33057.md) informed the press the US would be supplying [M142 HIMARS](147011.md) [multiple-launch rocket systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_rocket_launcher) to Ukraine.[^cite_note-62] Some analysts said HIMARS could be a "game-changer" in the war.[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-bCole-64] Under Secretary of Defense for Policy [Colin Kahl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Kahl) stated the US would be able to send more systems as the fighting evolved.[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-iAli-66]

On 10 June 2022, an official from the Ukrainian military said they were using 5,000 to 6,000 artillery rounds a day and would then be using 155-calibre. NATO-standard shells because all of their Soviet-era guns had been destroyed. The official said the Russians had transformed the war into an artillery duel that focused on the southeast of Ukraine.[^cite_note-iktgvs-67] On 12 June, a Ukrainian presidential advisor posted on social-media platform [Twitter](9988187.md) a list of weapons Ukraine needed to achieve "heavy weapons parity"; the top item was "1000 howitzers caliber 155 mm".[^cite_note-68] Ukraine said it had enough 155 mm ammunition but lacked the artillery to use it. According to [Oryxspioenkop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oryxspioenkop), only 250 howitzers had been promised or delivered.[^cite_note-69] On 13 June, a [Deutsche Welle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Welle) correspondent said the Ukrainian supply of Soviet-era ammunition had been exhausted and the country had only a dwindling supply obtained from friendly ex-Soviet countries.[^cite_note-dwn13.6-70] In June 2022 Germany declassified its list of military aid to Ukraine.[^cite_note-71] As of July 2022, [CNN](62028.md) reported recently declassified American intelligence suggested Iranians have given Russian forces [Shahed 129](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahed_129) UAV combat drones.[^cite_note-72]

Wikisource logo

English [Wikisource](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikisource) has original text related to this article:&#x20;

**[\$1 Billion in New U.S. Military Assistance for Ukraine](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/en:\$1%20Billion%20in%20New%20U.S.%20Military%20Assistance%20for%20Ukraine)**

By 2 August 2022, for the 16 US-supplied HIMARS systems in Ukraine, the US was providing more munitions—additional HIMARS rocket pods in monthly installments, as well as more 155-mm howitzer shells—at a cost of \$550 million for the 17th Presidential drawdown package.[^cite_note-2Aug2022-73] The 18th US presidential drawdown package, which was released on 8 Aug 2022, was a \$1 billion package including additional HIMARS rocket pods, 75,000 rounds of 155 mm artillery ammunition, 20 120 mm mortar systems and 20,000 rounds of 120 mm mortar ammunition, [National Advanced Surface-to-Air Missile Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Advanced_Surface-to-Air_Missile_System) (NASAMS),[^cite_note-nasamsImminent-74][^cite_note-logfas-75][^cite_note-nasamsSynchronized-76] 1,000 Javelins and hundreds of [AT4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT4) anti-tank weapons, 50 armored medical treatment vehicles, [Claymore mines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claymore_mine), [C4 explosives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C4_explosive), and medical supplies.[^cite_note-8Aug2022-77] The 19th US presidential drawdown package on 19 Aug 2022 was a \$775 million package that included additional HIMARS rocket pods; 16 105 mm howitzers with 36,000 artillery rounds to supplement the UK's past contributions of 105 mm howitzers; 1,000 anti-armor Javelins; 2,000 anti-armor rounds for the Swedish [Carl Gustaf 8.4 cm recoilless rifle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Gustaf_8.4_cm_recoilless_rifle); 1,500 tube-launched, optically tracked, wire-guided, anti-tank missiles ([BGM-71 TOWs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BGM-71_TOW)); additional [AGM-88 HARM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGM-88_HARM) air-launched[^cite_note-mig29sWithAgm88Harm-78]<sup>: 4\:17 </sup> anti-radiation missiles that home on radar sites; 15 ScanEagle UAVs to guide Ukrainian artillery; 40 [mine flail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mine_flail) vehicles to clear out minefields; 50 HMMWVs, tactical secure communication systems; demolition munitions; night vision devices; thermal imagery systems; optics; and laser rangefinders.[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-seekDestroy-80] Since 2021, the packages totaled \$10.7 billion by 19 August 2022.[^cite_note-19Aug2022-81][^cite_note-8Aug2022-77]

In September 2022, 800 combat drones manufactured by the Taiwanese [DronesVision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DronesVision) were transferred to Ukraine through Poland.[^cite_note-Taiwan_News_1-82][^cite_note-83] In November 2022, the UK announced it was donating to Ukraine three former [Royal Navy](26061.md) and [Royal Air Force](25679.md) [Sea King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_King_\(helicopter\)) helicopters .[^cite_note-84]

The [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War) has assessed the need for Western supplies was apparent in June 2022 and that, if commitments and preparations for delivery had been made at that time, Ukraine would have been ready to deploy the materiel in November or December 2022. Western nations committed to the delivery of tanks in January 2023. Ukraine was unable to take advantage of Russian military [depletion and disorganization](#Use_of_civilian_observers_for_aiding_Ukrainian_operations) following the successful Ukrainian counter-offenses at [Kharkiv](2788558.md) and [Kherson](1025861.md) in late 2022 due in part to the need to conserve limited stocks of Soviet equipment and in the absence of an expectation of Western replacements. This delay allowed Russia to reorganize and reinforce its armed forces, potentially prolonging the war.[^cite_note-85]

### United States' Ukraine security assistance package



US President [Joe Biden](145422.md) and Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) at the [G7 summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/50th_G7_summit) in Italy, 13 June 2024

On 24 August 2022, US President Joe Biden announced \$3 billion in aid for longer-term requirements by Ukraine, it was the largest aid package since the beginning of the Russian invasion. The money was released from a congressional funding source ([Ukraine Security Assistance Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Security_Assistance_Initiative) (USAI)) to allow the US government to procure weapons from industry, including [NASAMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASAMS) air defense units, [Puma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AeroVironment_RQ-20_Puma) drones, and [Vampire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_Precision_Kill_Weapon_System) counter-drone missiles.[^cite_note-86][^cite_note-ukrContractingFactSheet31Jan-87] The Vampire contract was not yet let as of November 2022, with delivery to Ukraine after mid-2023.[^cite_note-vampire2023-88] The longer-term deliveries of materiel included six additional NASAMS air-defense units and their attendant rounds for a total of eight units;[^cite_note-nasamsImminent-74][^cite_note-15SepFactSheet-89] up to 245,000 155 mm howitzer shells; up to 65,000 120 mm mortar rounds; up to 24 [counter-battery radars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-battery_radar), and the attendant training, maintenance, and sustainment.[^cite_note-usai2-90] By 24 August 2022, US aid since January 2021 exceeded \$13.5 billion.[^cite_note-totalAid-91] On 25 August 2022, President Zelenskyy thanked President Biden for the \$3 billion [USAI security aid package](#Ukraine_Security_Assistance_Initiative) he announced the previous day, and the \$3 billion World Bank financial-aid package for Ukraine.[^cite_note-worldBankPackage-92] On September 2, Biden requested from Congress \$13.7 billion "for equipment, intelligence support and direct budgetary support" to Ukraine.[^cite_note-jalonik-93][^cite_note-feldscher-94]

By August 2022, the United Kingdom had provided military aid to the value of £2.3bn (\$2.8bn). This included three [M270 Multiple Launch Rocket Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M270_Multiple_Launch_Rocket_System), some 5,000 [NLAW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NLAW) anti-tank missiles, "hundreds" of [Brimstone missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brimstone_\(missile\)), 120 armoured vehicles including [Mastiff Protected Patrol Vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cougar_\(MRAP\)), and heavy-lift drones.[^cite_note-95] Additionally, 10,000 Ukrainian soldiers were in an intensive, 120-day infantry training course at four bases in the UK.[^cite_note-96]

On 8 September 2022, US Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) announced \$2 billion in aid to Ukraine and eighteen partners in the defense industrial base.[^cite_note-tBlinken8sep22-97] In addition, US Secretary of Defense [Lloyd J. Austin III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_J._Austin_III) announced the 20th drawdown package; up to \$675 million for Ukraine military aid at the fifth [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group) meeting in Germany, and a discussion of initiatives for the respective industrial bases of the Defense Contact Group, for the long-term defense of Ukraine's sovereign territory.[^cite_note-jGaramone-98][^cite_note-lAustin8sep22-99] On 28 September, [William LaPlante](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_A._LaPlante), US [Under Secretary of Defense for Acquisition and Sustainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Under_Secretary_of_Defense_for_Acquisition_and_Sustainment) (USD (A\&S)), met in Brussels with 40 counterparts in the Ukraine Defense Contact Group. On the agenda was the identification of industrial suppliers of replacement materiel such as gun barrels, ball bearings, steel casings, and microchips, without which the existing military aid will eventually cease to function due to heavy use on the battlefield.[^cite_note-laplante1-100] LaPlante later noted a policy of "interoperability, but interchangeability, with multiple plants in multiple countries making identical items" will have a deterrent effect on those countries' adversaries and on those of Ukraine.[^cite_note-laplante2-101] Arms suppliers from Eastern Europe were also arming Ukraine using [Ukraine Defense Contacts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group).[^cite_note-eastEuArmsUkr-102] On 15 September 2022, Biden announced his 21st drawdown package, which was worth \$600 million in military aid to Ukraine in light of the [2022 Kharkiv counteroffensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Kharkiv_counteroffensive).[^cite_note-21stDrawdown-103]

On 28 September 2022, the US Department of Defense announced a Ukraine Security Assistance Initiative (USAI) package worth up to \$1.1 billion, which would purchase 18 additional HIMARS systems and their associated rockets from vendors in the future.[^cite_note-usai28Sep22-104] By 28 September, 16 HIMARS systems drawn from the US and another 10 equivalent systems from the allies were in service in Ukraine. This USAI package was also to include 150 [High Mobility Multipurpose Wheeled Vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Mobility_Multipurpose_Wheeled_Vehicle) (Humvees or HMMWVs), 150 tactical vehicles, 20 multi-mission radars, explosive ordnance disposal equipment, body armor and tactical secure communications systems, surveillance systems, and optics.[^cite_note-usai28Sep22-104] This long-term package also included training for Ukrainian troops, maintenance, and sustainment, totaling \$16.2 billion in aid since the beginning of the 2022 invasion.[^cite_note-usai28Sep22-104]

On 20 February 2023, during an [unexpected visit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_visit_by_Joe_Biden_to_Ukraine) to [Kyiv](585629.md), US President Biden announced a half-billion dollar aid package for Ukraine,[^cite_note-bidenInKiev-105] including artillery ammunition, more [FGM-148 Javelins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FGM-148_Javelin), and [howitzers](14392.md).[^cite_note-bidenDetails-106][^cite_note-UkraineFactSheet20Feb23-107][^cite_note-29billionAid-108] As of September 2023, US Security aid to Ukraine since Russia's full-scale invasion in February 2022 had exceeded \$43 billion.[^cite_note-109]

On 11 October 2023, following [U.S. budget funding difficulty for 2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuing_resolution#2024_U.S._federal_budget) in the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) and the [removal of its speaker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Removal_of_Kevin_McCarthy_as_Speaker_of_the_House), [U.S. National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._National_Security_Council) spokesman [John Kirby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kirby_\(admiral\)) said support provided to Ukraine was not going to be indefinite. Funding was now on a short-term [continuing resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuing_resolution). Kirby said: "on the Ukraine funding, we're coming near to the end of the rope ... today we announced \$200 million, and we'll keep that aid going as long as we can, but it's not going to be indefinite". The administration was in discussions with members of Congress about additional funding for Ukraine.[^cite_note-independent-20231012-110][^cite_note-msn-20231012-111][^cite_note-whitehouse-20231011-112]

#### Lend-Lease for Ukraine

On 1 October 2022 [Lend-Lease for Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Democracy_Defense_Lend-Lease_Act_of_2022) came into effect.[^cite_note-lendLease-114] A proposal to administer US security assistance as part of [EUCOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EUCOM) was under consideration at [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon).[^cite_note-newEucomCmd-115] This plan would systematize the services currently being provided to Ukraine on an *ad hoc* basis, and would provide a long-term vehicle for countering Russian plans under the provisions of the Lend-Lease act, and for coordinating Allied aid for Ukraine's defense with Ukrainian requests at a single point of contact in [Wiesbaden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wiesbaden), Germany.[^cite_note-newEucomCmd-115][^cite_note-orenBarbara-116]

On 4 October 2022, the 22nd presidential drawdown from US stocks to Ukraine, \$625 million in security assistance, included a tailored package consisting of four more HIMARS systems and their associated rockets; 16 more [M777](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M777) 155 mm howitzers and 75,000 155 mm rounds; 500 [M982 Excalibur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M982_Excalibur) precision-guided 155 mm rounds; 1,000 155 mm rounds of remote anti-armor mine systems; 16 more 105 mm [M119 howitzers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M119_howitzer); 30,000 120 mm mortar rounds; 200 mine-resistant, ambush-protected vehicles ([MRAPs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MRAP)); 200,000 rounds of small arms ammunition; and [Claymore mines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claymore_mine). The package responded to Ukrainian ammunition-consumption rates during their latest offensives; according to [Laura Cooper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Cooper), a US DoD deputy assistant secretary of defense, more aid would be forthcoming .[^cite_note-22ndPda-117] So far, the security assistance to Ukraine had totalled \$16.8 billion to Ukraine.[^cite_note-22ndPda-117]

On 14 October 2022, the 23rd presidential drawdown from US stocks provided Ukraine \$725 million in security assistance, including additional rounds for high-mobility artillery rocket systems (HIMARS), 23,000 155 mm howitzer rounds, 500 precision-guided 155 mm artillery rounds, 5,000 155 mm rounds of remote anti-armor mine (RAAM) systems, 5,000 anti-tank weapons, [high-speed anti-radiation missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_anti-radiation_missile) (HARMs), more than 200 HMMWVs; small arms and more than 2,000,000 rounds of small-arms ammunition, and medical supplies.[^cite_note-23rdPda-118] The security assistance to Ukraine since January 2021 had totalled \$18.2 billion.[^cite_note-23rdPda-118]

On 31 January 2023, Reuters reported the US presidential drawdown authority had allocated more than \$400 million in materiel, including a new weapon, the [GLSDB](#GLSDB) available under USAI.[^cite_note-usaiPda-119][^cite_note-trainingMoreManeuver-120][^cite_note-glsdbImminent-121] On 3 February 2023, another presidential drawdown package was announced.[^cite_note-vergun3febOps-122] The package included additional ammunition for HiMARS, 155 mm artillery rounds, and 120 mm mortar rounds; 190 heavy machine guns with thermal imagery sights and associated ammunition to counter unmanned aerial systems; 181 Mine Resistant Ambush Protected vehicles; 250 Javelin anti-armor systems; 2,000 anti-armor rockets; Claymore anti-personnel munitions; demolition munitions; and cold-weather equipment, helmets, and other field equipment.[^cite_note-vergun3febOps-122]

### Proposal for a Kyiv Security Compact

&#x20;In September 2022, former NATO Secretary General [Anders Fogh Rasmussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anders_Fogh_Rasmussen) delivered a proposal for a long-lasting [Kyiv Security Compact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_Security_Compact) to Ukraine's President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md); the proposed compact involved legally binding security guarantees for Ukraine from a coalition of Western countries to bolster its ability to repel Russian attacks through extensive joint training, the provision of advanced defensive weapons systems, and support to develop the country's defense industry.[^cite_note-123]

### Aid in construction of a missile defense system

Missile-defense systems arrived in Ukraine piecemeal;[^cite_note-piecemealAmd-127] in Brussels on 12 October 2022, US Army General [Mark Milley](38091005.md) suggested to the [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group)[^cite_note-UkraineContactGroup-50] the allies of Ukraine "chip in to help Ukraine rebuild and sustain an integrated air and missile defense system" from the contributed air-and-missile defense-system materiel.[^cite_note-ukraineMissileDefenseSystem-126] Ukraine would need to link together and integrate its existing materiel and radars with "command and control and communication systems".[^cite_note-ukraineMissileDefenseSystem-126][^cite_note-udcgNov22-128]<sup>: 15\:15 </sup>[^cite_note-linkC3-129]<sup>: minute 20\:25 </sup>[^cite_note-UkrAirDefense-131]

On 12 October 2022, German Defense Minister [Christine Lambrecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christine_Lambrecht) announced an IRIS-T air-defense system had arrived in Ukraine, with three more forthcoming in 2023.[^cite_note-1stirist-132] On 28 October, The Pentagon announced the 24th presidential drawdown of materiel worth \$275 million; the security assistance totalled \$18.5 billion to Ukraine since January 2021.[^cite_note-stripes28Oct22-133] The aid included 500 Excalibur precision-guided 155 mm artillery rounds, 2,000 155 mm remote anti-armor mine systems, more than 1,300 anti-armor weapons, more than 2.75 million rounds of small-arms ammunition, more HIMARS rockets, 125 Humvees, and four satellite-communications antennas for Ukraine's [command and control](7092305.md) systems, as well as training for operation of the [NASAMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASAMS) units.[^cite_note-nasamsImminent-74] Two NASAMS units arrived in Ukraine on 7 November 2022.[^cite_note-stripes28Oct22-133][^cite_note-2nasams-134][^cite_note-nasams16Nov22-137]

### Security Assistance Group Ukraine (SAGU)

By 21 July 2022, the [United States European Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_European_Command) (EUCOM) Control Center-Ukraine/International Donor Coordination Centre (ECCU/IDCC), a joint cell that was formed in March 2022, had trained 1,500 Ukrainian Armed Forces personnel on coalition-donated equipment.[^cite_note-eccuIdcc-138] By 4 November 2022, the equipment shipments and training measures of the Ukraine Contact Group had become repeatable enough to systematize in a [Security Assistance Group Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Security_Assistance_Initiative#SAGU) (SAGU), which was based in Wiesbaden, Germany.[^cite_note-orenBarbara-116][^cite_note-dougWare-139][^cite_note-megMyers-140]

On 4 November 2022, the Pentagon announced a \$400 million [Ukraine Security Assistance Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Security_Assistance_Initiative) (USAI) security assistance package to refurbish 45 [T-72](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-72) tanks from the Czech Republic with "advanced optics, communications and armor packages";[^cite_note-usai400million-141] the package also included 1,100 [Phoenix Ghost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenix_Ghost) tactical [unmanned aerial systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unmanned_aerial_system) (UASs) and 40 armored riverine boats.[^cite_note-usai400million-141] The combined additional aid amounted to 90 more T-72s by year-end 2022, 250 [M1117 armored security vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M1117_armored_security_vehicle) furnished for the first time, and refurbished HAWK missiles from the Czech Republic that will serve on the HAWK launchers from Spain. On 10 November, the \$400-million aid announcement was clarified; four Stinger-based air defense [AN/TWQ-1 Avengers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/TWQ-1_Avenger) to counter the Iranian drones were provided to Ukraine for the first time, as were additional HIMARS rockets, 10,000 mortar rounds, thousands of 155 mm howitzer rounds, 400 grenade launchers, 100 Humvees, 20 million rounds of small-arms ammunition, and cold-weather gear.[^cite_note-avengers-142] In November 2022, the 20 HIMARS launchers drawn from US stocks were still in service; Poland and other countried had provided hundreds of [T-72s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-72); the USAI security assistance totalled \$18.9 billion to Ukraine since January 2021.[^cite_note-usai400Mm-143]

On 15 November 2022, the US [White House Office of Management and Budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_Office_of_Management_and_Budget) asked Congress for an additional \$38 billion for aid to Ukraine in fiscal year 2023.[^cite_note-38billionMore-144] The supplemental funding request included \$21.7 billion in security assistance, \$14.5 billion in [US State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_State_Department) sources and USAID sources to be provided to Ukraine's government, humanitarian relief and global food security, and a \$900 million request for the [Department of Health and Human Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Health_and_Human_Services) to "provide standard assistance health care and support services to Ukrainian parolees". In addition, a \$626 million [United States Department of Energy](62866.md) request would aid nuclear security at the power plant in [Zaporizhzhia](1873579.md).[^cite_note-38billionMore-144] The White House also requested Congress to grant \$7 billion in additional presidential drawdown authority from existing Defense Department materiel.[^cite_note-38billionMore-144] If Congress was to grant this fourth request, the total aid to Ukraine would be \$104 billion in less than a year.[^cite_note-38billionMore-144]

On 17 November 2022, it was reported Israel had approved the transfer of weapons systems with Israeli parts via NATO countries, including the UK. These included advanced fire-control and electro-optic systems. It also agreed to buy strategic materials for the Ukrainian armed forces.[^cite_note-145]

On 23 November 2022 the Pentagon announced its 26th drawdown package of up to \$400 million in aid,[^cite_note-26thPkg-146] consisting of HIMARS rockets, [AGM-88 high-speed anti-radiation missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGM-88_HARM), 200 precision-guided 155 mm artillery [M982 Excalibur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M982_Excalibur) rounds, 150 heavy machine guns to shoot down drones, additional [NASAMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASAMS) missiles for air defense, 150 Humvees, over 100 additional light tactical vehicles, over 200 electrical generators, 20 million rounds of small-arms ammunition, and spare parts for 105 mm howitzers.[^cite_note-26thPkg-146]

On 9 December 2022, the Pentagon announced the US president had authorized the 27th drawdown package of up to \$275 million in aid to Ukraine for additional HIMARS rockets, 80,000 155 mm artillery rounds, [counter-unmanned aerial systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unmanned_aerial_system) (UASs), counter-air defense equipment, Humvees, ambulances and medical equipment, nearly 150 generators, and field equipment.[^cite_note-27thPkg-147]

On 21 December 2022, Antony Blinken of the US State Department announced the 28th drawdown of aid for Ukraine, a \$1 billion package consisting of a [Patriot missile battery](52024.md):[^cite_note-adaUse-148] the Pentagon also announced \$850 million of security assistance for Ukraine under its Ukraine Security Assistance Initiative. Training in the use of these Patriot missiles will be required for Ukraine's troops.[^cite_note-28thPackage-149][^cite_note-hawkAdaPatriot-150][^cite_note-patriotTrainingFtSill-151] The materiel also included:&#x20;

- [JDAM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JDAM) kits

  [^cite_note-jdams-152]

  &#x20;for precision aerial munitions

- ammunition for [HIMARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIMARS) High Mobility Artillery Rocket Systems

- 500 [precision-guided 155 mm artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precision-guided_munition) rounds

- 10 120 mm mortar systems and 10,000 120 mm mortar rounds

- 10 82 mm mortar systems

- 10 60 mm mortar systems

- 37 Cougar Mine Resistant Ambush Protected vehicles ([MRAPs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MRAP))

- 120 High Mobility Multipurpose Wheeled Vehicles ([Humvees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humvee))

- six armored utility trucks

- high-speed anti-radiation missiles

- 2,700 grenade launchers and small arms

- Claymore anti-personnel munitions

- demolition munitions and equipment

- night-vision devices and optics

- tactical secure communications systems and

- body armor and other field equipment.

The USAI (from industry rather than from [United States Department of Defense](7279897.md) stocks) were:

- 45,000 152 mm artillery rounds

- 20,000 122 mm artillery rounds

- 50,000 122 mm GRAD rockets

- 100,000 rounds of 125 mm tank ammunition

- satellite communications terminals and services, and

- funding for training, maintenance and sustainment.

  [^cite_note-28thPackDetail-153]

  [^cite_note-adaUse-148]

Wikinews logo

Wikinews has related news:&#x20;

- [ France to be first nation to donate Western armored vehicles to Ukraine](https://en.wikinews.org/wiki/France%20to%20be%20first%20nation%20to%20donate%20Western%20armored%20vehicles%20to%20Ukraine)

On 6 January 2023, Chancellor Scholz and President Biden announced the contribution of Marder and [Bradley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_Fighting_Vehicle) armored fighting vehicles from Germany and the US, respectively;[^cite_note-marderBfv-155] [President Macron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_Macron) had announced France's contribution of [AMX-10 RC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMX-10_RC) armored fighting vehicles two days earlier.[^cite_note-amx10-156] About 50 Bradley Fighting Vehicles were in the US drawdown package; several dozen AMX-10s were available from France. The 29th US drawdown amounted to \$2.85 billion in aid;[^cite_note-marderBfv-155] the US aid package and provided \$200 million in foreign military financing for Ukraine.[^cite_note-garamone6Jan2023-157][^cite_note-suddenInflux-158] The Bradley package included 50 MRAPs, 138 Humvees, 500 [TOW missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TOW_missile), and 250,000 rounds of 25 mm ammunition.[^cite_note-garamone6Jan2023-157] The US also announced the contribution of 18 [Paladin self-propelled 155 mm howitzers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M109_howitzer) for the first time[^cite_note-garamone6Jan2023-157] as well as 100 additional [M113 armored personnel carriers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M113_armored_personnel_carrier)[^cite_note-29thDrawdown-159] and 70,000 additional 155 mm howitzer rounds. The package also included 4,000 [Zuni rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuni_rocket) to be fired from Ukrainian aircraft and [RIM-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RIM-7) missiles modified to be fired from Ukrainian [Buk launchers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buk_missile_system).[^cite_note-zuniJdam-160][^cite_note-ware29thDrawdown-161]

Scholz also announced a German [Patriot missile battery](52024.md) would go to Ukraine in a billion-dollar package.[^cite_note-patriotTrainingFtSill-151][^cite_note-aRoque-162] as well as 40 Marder vehicles.[^cite_note-40marders-163] On 17 January 2023, Netherlands Prime Minister [Mark Rutte](4321056.md) announced he would send Patriot missile launchers to Ukraine, the third country to donate Patriots;[^cite_note-3rdPatriot-164] two launchers arrived in April 2023.[^cite_note-dutchLaunchers-165]

### Swiss blocks on military exports to Ukraine

In mid 2022, Switzerland vetoed Denmark's request to send Swiss-made [Piranha III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mowag_Piranha) armored vehicles to Ukraine. Switzerland requires countries that purchase Swiss arms to request permission to re-export them.[^cite_note-Reuters2-166] Switzerland has also vetoed German requests to donate tank munitions to Ukraine.[^cite_note-Reuters1-167][^cite_note-Reuters4-168] Switzerland has also vetoed Poland's,[^cite_note-Reuters3-169] Spain's[^cite_note-SWI_swissinfo.ch_2023-170][^cite_note-Switzerland_Times_2023-171] and other countries' requests.

### Additional military provisions for Ukraine

On 17 October 2022 the European Union approved €500 million (\$486 million) in weapons for Ukraine,[^cite_note-blejean-172] and a two-year training mission initially under the command of France's Vice Admiral [Hervé Bléjean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herv%C3%A9_Bl%C3%A9jean) for 15,000 Ukrainian troops.[^cite_note-blejean-172] The training would be held in Germany and Poland, and would be open to other nations. The planned training cost was nearly €107 million.[^cite_note-blejean-172] The EU doubled the training commitment to 30,000 troops.[^cite_note-euDoublesTraining-173]

On 11 January 2023, Poland announced it would provide a company of 12 [Leopard 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopard_2) tanks[^cite_note-euTanks-174] to Ukraine;[^cite_note-roqueMartin-175] on 14 January 2023, the United Kingdom announced it would give Ukraine 14 [Challenger 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Challenger_2) tanks,[^cite_note-176] compared to the [4 November 2022](#4nov2022T72s) entry in which 90 T-72 tanks were being refurbished by Czech suppliers, with estimated delivery by year-end 2022. Lt. Gen. (Retired) [Ben Hodges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Hodges) assessed the contributions up to January 2023 as the equivalent of an armored brigade.[^cite_note-ironFist-177][^cite_note-abct-178] Hodges said an armored division was the goal;[^cite_note-armoredDiv-179][^cite_note-ciaFeb2022-180][^cite_note-armoredDivReq-182] the 11 January aid being:

- 26 Czech [Dana M2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/152_mm_SpGH_DANA) self-propelled howitzers

- 18 US [Paladin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M109_Paladin) self-propelled howitzers

- 30 UK [AS-90](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AS-90) self-propelled guns

- and unspecified number of French [AMX-10 RC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMX-10_RC) armored reconnaissance vehicles

- 40 German [Marder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marder_\(infantry_fighting_vehicle\)) infantry fighting vehicles (IFVs)

- 50 US Bradley IFVs

  [^cite_note-combinedArms-184]

- 14 Challenger 2 tanks

- 12 Leopard 2 tanks, and

- 90 [T-72](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-72) tanks.

  [^cite_note-petraeus-185]

The eighth meeting[^cite_note-8thUdcg-186][^cite_note-8thMeet20Jan2023-187] of the Ukraine Defense Contact Group gathered at [Ramstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramstein-Miesenbach), Germany, on 18–20 January 2023, where the new German defense minister was introduced to the group.[^cite_note-pistorius-188] Nine countries pledged support:[^cite_note-nineCountries-189][^cite_note-Anusauskas-190][^cite_note-noAssentYet-191]

- Canada pledged to supply Ukraine with 200 Canadian [APCs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armored_personnel_carrier), National Advanced Surface-to-Air Missile System ([NASAMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASAMS)), 39 other [armoured support vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LAV_6), anti-tank weapons, and [M777](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M777) howitzers. Canada was ranked fifth by materiel volume behind Poland, Germany, the UK, and the US.

  [^cite_note-canada5th-192]

- Czech Republic pledged to increase industrial production for large-caliber munitions, howitzers, and APCs

  [^cite_note-nineCountries-189]

- Denmark pledged training worth €600 million for Ukrainian soldiers

  [^cite_note-nineCountries-189]

- Estonia pleadged an unspecified quantity of 155 mm FH-70 and 122 mm D-30 howitzers, thousands of 155 mm artillery ammunition rounds, support vehicles, [Carl-Gustaf M2 anti-tank grenade launchers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Gustaf_8.4_cm_recoilless_rifle) with ammunition rounds worth €113 million, and training for Ukrainian soldiers

  [^cite_note-nineCountries-189]

- Latvia pledged tens of [MANPADS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MANPADS) Stingers, two M-17 helicopters, tens of UAVs, spare parts for [M109](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M109) howitzers, and training for 2,000 Ukrainian soldiers

  [^cite_note-nineCountries-189]

- Lithuania pledged a support package worth €125 million, two Mi-8 helicopters worth €85 million, counter-drones, optics, thermo-visual devices, and drones worth €40 million, and a €2 million donation to UK international fund for heavy weaponry

  [^cite_note-nineCountries-189]

- Poland pledged [S-60](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AZP_S-60) anti-aircraft guns with 70,000 pieces of ammunition; it had already donated 42 infantry fighting vehicles, training packages for two mechanized battalions, more 155 mm [Krab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AHS_Krab) howitzers, and ammunition

  [^cite_note-nineCountries-189]

- Slovakia pledged to Increase production of howitzers, de-mining equipment and ammunition, and to provide training for Ukrainian soldiers

- The UK's largest aid package thus far included 14 [Challenger 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Challenger_2) tanks, 30 [AS-90](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AS-90) self-propelled guns, [Bulldog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FV432) APCs, breachers, bridgelayers, dozens of drones, 100,000 artillery rounds, [Starstreak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starstreak) air-defense missiles, [GMLRS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M270_Multiple_Launch_Rocket_System) ammunition, 600 [Brimstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brimstone_\(missile\)) anti-tank munitions, and training for Ukrainian troops to operate this materiel.
