  [^cite_note-fmcAifv-201]

### Additional tanks for Ukraine

In contrast to [attrition warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attrition_warfare), in which tanks are often used as fire support, a potential switch to [maneuver warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maneuver_warfare) is assumed to require tanks.[^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203][^cite_note-usingUkrTanks-204] Although Poland and Finland had separately agreed to send Ukraine 12 Leopard 2 tanks each,[^cite_note-dw19Jan2023-205] Germany must agree to license their transfer.[^cite_note-pistoriusAustin-206][^cite_note-hunderChambers-207][^cite_note-epstein-208] US Secretary Austin has met with Germany's Defence Minister Pistorius on securing Leopard 2 tanks for Ukraine.[^cite_note-pistoriusAustin-206][^cite_note-noAssentYet-191] As of 22 January 2023, Germany would not block the export of Leopard 2 tanks by other contact-group nations.[^cite_note-exportsOK-209][^cite_note-armoredDivReq-182]

On 20 January 2023, the Netherlands offered [F-16s](11642.md)[^cite_note-f16sLeopard2s-210][^cite_note-bremerGrieco-211] and Leopard 2s.[^cite_note-f16sLeopard2s-210] The offers were conditional on mutual agreements by multiple nations;[^cite_note-noAssentYet-191] Germany will not block the export of Leopard 2s (by Poland, Finland,[^cite_note-dw19Jan2023-205] Denmark,[^cite_note-exportsOK-209] Netherlands)[^cite_note-f16sLeopard2s-210] to Ukraine.[^cite_note-exportsOK-209] Poland had requested approval to export Leopard 2s to Ukraine.[^cite_note-PoReqsExport-212] On 25 January 2023, the US agreed to send tanks to Ukraine under the auspices of the USAI in a \$400 million package; 31 [M1 Abrams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M1_Abrams) tanks—an entire Ukraine tank battalion—[^cite_note-ukrTankBattalion-213] and eight [M88 Recovery Vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M88_Recovery_Vehicle)[^cite_note-m88explainer-214] would not arrive in Ukraine for months.[^cite_note-roque-215][^cite_note-roqSjf-216][^cite_note-tankReversal-217][^cite_note-usArmyOptions-218][^cite_note-usAbramsDeliv-222]

Germany also agreed to send more than 12 Leopard 2s to Ukraine, and to support the donation with ammunition and training in Germany. Germany also agreed to approve the re-export of Leopard 2s from other nations to Ukraine.[^cite_note-apaAz-223][^cite_note-plichta-224][^cite_note-tnsr123-225] The goal was to send 80 Leopard 2s to Ukraine.[^cite_note-80leopard2s-226] By 25 January 2023, the list of nations willing to send tanks to Ukraine had grown.[^cite_note-232] On 23 February, Finland announced its contribution would include three pieces of Leopard 2 mine-clearing tanks built on the Leopard 2A4 platform.[^cite_note-233]

On 7 February 2023, the European community determined industrial manufacturers held 178 Leopard 1 tanks that could be refurbished and provided to Ukraine,[^cite_note-leopard1-234] with 20-25 Leopard 1A5 tanks available in 2023. The remainder could be delivered in 2024, assuming Germany, the Netherlands, and Denmark fund them.[^cite_note-23apr2023Axe-235][^cite_note-leopard1-234][^cite_note-randProjection-239]

On 24 February 2023, Poland delivered four [Leopard 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopard_2)A4 tanks to Ukraine,[^cite_note-leopardsPoSe-240][^cite_note-ukrEvals-241] 10 more are to arrive by 9 March 2023;[^cite_note-10moreLeopards-242] Sweden has announced it intended to donate up to 10 Leopard 2A5 tanks.[^cite_note-leopardsPoSe-240] When marshaled, the contributions from Poland, Canada, Norway, and Spain suffice to field a battalion of Leopard 2A4s.[^cite_note-1battalion2A4s-243] Poland also gave Ukraine 60 [PT-91 tanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PT-91_Twardy).[^cite_note-60pt91s-244][^cite_note-usAbramsDeliv-222] On 22 April 2023, six Leopard 2A4s and 20 heavy armored transport vehicles were *en route* from Spain to Ukraine; the sea-borne part of the route would take six days.[^cite_note-spainLeosEnroute-245] Spain also trained 40 tank crew members and 15 mechanics from Ukraine.[^cite_note-spainLeosEnroute-245] On 20 April 2023, Netherlands and Denmark announced a joint contribution of 14 restored Leopard 2A4 tanks to Ukraine for early 2024.[^cite_note-21apr2023-246] Over the next year, Ukraine will be receiving 85 2A4s.[^cite_note-23apr2023Axe-235]

As of 21 April 2023, the coalition had provided more than 230 tanks and more than 1,550 armored vehicles.[^cite_note-21apr2023-246] On 20 September 2023, the Germany's federal government updated its list of military support[^cite_note-materiel2054992-247] for Ukraine from which materiel is supplied, and from which the supplies of the German military are replenished. The updates included spare parts for [MCT WISENT 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MCT_WISENT_1?action=edit\&redlink=1), which are tanks with mine-clearing capability, as well as four additional [HX81 trucks and trailers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RMMV_HX_range_of_tactical_trucks) and 12 additional [Mercedes-Benz Zetros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-Benz_Zetros).[^cite_note-materiel2054992-247]

Ten M1A1 Abrams tanks arrived in Ukraine on 25 September 2023,[^cite_note-nyt25Sep-248] armed with heavy, dense armor-piercing 120 mm shells made of [depleted uranium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Depleted_uranium), to be followed by 21 more as part of the 46th drawdown package from the US.[^cite_note-depletedU-249][^cite_note-10M1A1-250] The tank battalion will likely be held in reserve in undisclosed locations until its use can be decisive (rather than dissipated in full frontal assaults).[^cite_note-nyt25Sep-248][^cite_note-21Sep2023FactSheet-251] The M1A1 Abrams tanks appear to be committed to the defence of Avdiivka as of 23 February 2024.[^cite_note-axe23Feb2024-252]

### Training in combined arms operations

By January 2023, battalion-level training in combined arms[^cite_note-combinedArms-184][^cite_note-using300Tanks-254] maneuver for Ukraine was underway.[^cite_note-rfeRl-255][^cite_note-ukrTankBattalion-213] By mid-2023, 2,000 Ukrainian soldiers would be trained in France; in Poland, 150 additional French instructors would augment the training of 600 Ukrainian soldiers per month—up from 280 soldiers per month.[^cite_note-franceAirDefense-200] Similar training came from Canada,[^cite_note-trainingFromCan-257] Denmark, Estonia, Latvia, Poland, Slovakia, Germany, the UK, and the US. The EU doubled its training commitment to 30,000 troops.[^cite_note-euDoublesTraining-173] In February, a battalion of Ukrainian troops completed five weeks of unit training in [combined arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_arms) in Germany.[^cite_note-grafenwohr-258][^cite_note-Leo2Trai-259][^cite_note-basicTactics-183] Training of the experienced Ukrainian tankers could take a third to a half the time needed for conscripted troops, estimated Lt. Gen. (Retired) Ben Hodges.[^cite_note-Leo2Trai-259][^cite_note-sjf123-260]To reach a state of readiness, maintenance of the donated Leopard 2 tanks would take weeks.[^cite_note-sjf123-260][^cite_note-fsGady-261] The troops could be ready by March 2023.[^cite_note-sjf123-260][^cite_note-euTanks-174][^cite_note-using300Tanks-254][^cite_note-usingUkrTanks-204] [^cite_note-trainInEstonia-263]

As of February 2023, the US was planning a HIMARS training center in Europe.[^cite_note-himarsTctr-264]

US troops have trained more than 7,000 Ukrainian soldiers as of 31 March 2023;[^cite_note-ukrTrainingUpdate-265] overall, 26 nations are currently training more than 11,000 Ukrainian soldiers.[^cite_note-ukrTrainingUpdate-265] As of 21 April, more than 11,000 Ukrainian soldiers were in training or had finished courses in Germany; an additional 2,250 Ukrainian soldiers (four motorized infantry battalions) were undertaking combined-arms training in Germany.[^cite_note-21apr2023-246] As of 21 April 2023 the US had trained seven mechanized Ukrainian battalions, three Stryker fighting vehicle battalions, three Bradley fighting vehicle battalions, and one motorized infantry battalion.[^cite_note-21apr2023Skove-266] By 21 April, another motorized infantry battalion had arrived in Germany for training. An armored battalion of 31 M1A1 Abrams tanks arrived in Germany in mid-May.[^cite_note-m1A1Abrams15May-267] The tanks were for training a Ukraine tank battalion of 250 soldiers;[^cite_note-m1AbramsAug23-221] these tanks will not be going to Ukraine but another 31 M1A1s were being refurbished for Ukraine and would arrive there by late 2023.[^cite_note-21apr2023Skove-266][^cite_note-usAbramsDeliv-222]

By 9 June 2023, the training in combined-arms operations, which involves the repair of armoured vehicles, was demonstrated during the June counteroffensive.[^cite_note-armorRepair-268][^cite_note-269] Ukrainian troops used night-vision goggles to gain a relative advantage over the Russian troops,[^cite_note-armorRepair-268][^cite_note-270] and used [Remote Anti-Armor Mine Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remote_Anti-Armor_Mine_System) to mine the roads between first and second Russian lines of defense.[^cite_note-armorRepair-268][^cite_note-271]

By 18 July 2023 the [Ukraine Defense Contact Group (UDCG)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group) "\[had] trained 17 brigade combat teams, 63,000 Ukrainian troops, and training continues", according to Gen. [Mark Milley](38091005.md).[^cite_note-trainingByUdcg-272]

[Oleksii Reznikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksii_Reznikov) called for the training of [sappers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sapper) because Ukraine has become the most-heavily mined country in the world.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-trainingNeeded23-274]

### Ukraine's air and missile defense

As of 14 February 2023, to help better defend against Russia's missile attacks, Ukraine's air-defense capabilities included equipment to integrate Western air-defense launchers, missiles, and radars with Ukraine's air-defense systems. These consisted of:

- four air surveillance radars

- two [Patriot batteries](52024.md);

  [^cite_note-dutchLaunchers-165]

  [^cite_note-patriotTrainingFtSill-151]

  [^cite_note-patriotArrives-275]

  [^cite_note-19apr2023-276]

- HAWK air-defense launchers

- satellite communications terminals and services

- [IRIS-T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRIS-T) systems from Germany

- a medium-range [SAMP/T](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aster_\(missile_family\)) from France

- Ukraine's [S-300s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-300_missile_system) and [MANPADs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MANPAD) (including [Starstreaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starstreak) and [FIM-92 Stingers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIM-92_Stinger))

- [AN/TPQ-36](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/TPQ-36) and [AN/TPQ-53](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/TPQ-53) radars already in Ukraine's inventory as [Counter-battery radars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-battery_radar); [NASAMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASAMS) systems (at least 3, with 6 more coming)

- [AN/TWQ-1 Avengers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AN/TWQ-1_Avenger)

- 340 heavy machine guns with thermal imagery sights and associated ammunition.

On 2 February 2023, France and Ukraine announced a complete medium-range air defense system.[^cite_note-franceAirDefense-200] (an [Aster 30 Block 1NT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aster_\(missile_family\)) SAMP/T system)[^cite_note-feb23Udcg-277]

On 3 February 2023, concurrent with a \$400 million drawdown package, the USAI authority announced additional air-and-missile-defense capability for Ukraine;[^cite_note-vergun3febOps-122][^cite_note-ukrFactSheet3feb2023-16][^cite_note-ukrContractingFactSheet31Jan-87] the package authorized up to \$1.725 billion in materiel. Under USAI, the [United States Department of Defense](7279897.md) would provide Ukraine with:

- Two [HAWK air defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIM-23_Hawk)

  [^cite_note-hawkAdaPatriot-150]

  &#x20;firing units;

- Anti-aircraft guns and ammunition;

- Equipment to integrate Western air defense launchers, missiles and radars with Ukraine's air defense systems;

- Equipment to sustain Ukraine's existing air defense capabilities;

- Air defense generators;

- Counter-unmanned aerial systems;

- Four air surveillance radars;

- 20 counter-mortar radars;

- Spare parts for counter-artillery radars;

- [AeroVironment RQ-20 Puma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AeroVironment_RQ-20_Puma) unmanned aerial systems;

- Precision-guided rockets;

- Secure communications equipment;

- Medical supplies, and

- funding for training, maintenance, and sustainment.

  [^cite_note-vergun3febOps-122]

  [^cite_note-davidPetraeus-278]

By 3 February 2023, US aid to Ukraine since January 2021 exceeded \$29.3 billion;[^cite_note-ukrFactSheet3feb2023-16] since 2014 US aid to Ukraine has exceeded \$32 billion.[^cite_note-ukrFactSheet3feb2023-16] French industrial aid to Ukraine's air defense was funded by a €200-million line designed by France for this purpose, having drawn half the fund so far.[^cite_note-franceAirDefense-200]

A new weapon, the [Ground Launched Small Diameter Bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground_Launched_Small_Diameter_Bomb) (GLSDB) was proposed for Ukraine in November 2022.[^cite_note-nov2022Glsdb-279] GLSDB has a range of 150 km (93 miles) and is funded by USAI.[^cite_note-usaiPda-119][^cite_note-ryder3Feb23-280] As the \$2.17 billion package includes a USAI component, that USAI component was not immediately available.[^cite_note-ryder3Feb23-280] GLSDB passed its tests and could show up in Ukraine's inventory as early as 31 January 2024.[^cite_note-ryder30Jan24-281][^cite_note-250pounderM26-282]

Canada and seventeen European nations have agreed to share [intelligence, surveillance, and reconnaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligence,_surveillance,_and_reconnaissance) (ISR) data which is to be gathered by satellites under the Allied Persistent Surveillance from Space Initiative (APSS).[^cite_note-apssi-283] A letter of intent for APSS was signed in Brussels on 15 February 2023; APSS begins operations in 2025.[^cite_note-apssi-283] The US [National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geospatial-Intelligence_Agency) (NGA) and the [National Reconnaissance Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Reconnaissance_Office) (NRO) had urged commercial satellite firms to provide ISR data to Ukraine.[^cite_note-apssi-283][^cite_note-ukrc2-284]

&#x20;On 24 February 2023, the US Department of Defense announced a USAI package for Ukraine worth \$2 billion: additional Unmanned Aerial Systems (UAS), as well as counter-UASs (counter-drones); additional HIMARS ammunition; additional 155 mm artillery ammunition; Mine clearing equipment; Secure communications support equipment; and Funding for training, maintenance, and sustainment.[^cite_note-1yearLater-285] This signifies the beginning of a contracting process with the US defense industrial base rather than the previous drawdowns from materiel stocks.[^cite_note-1yearLater-285] The US and multiple European nations are providing [§training in combined arms operations](#Training_in_combined_arms_operations) for Ukraine, in order to better use the aid being supplied in the future.

In December 2022, [NATO planning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO%E2%80%93Russia_relations#Breakdown_of_relations,_military_build-up_and_confrontation_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)) for the western flank of the NATO alliance had 8 battlegroup-sized [Rapid Reaction Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_Reaction_Force) stationed in Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, Poland, Slovakia, Hungary, Romania, and Bulgaria (listed north to south).[^cite_note-natoBattleGroupsWest-286] NATO planners alerted the member nations that up to 300,000 troops could be deployed to the western flank, in a 30-day period.[^cite_note-300kTroopDepl-287] On 17 March 2023, the [European Defence Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Defence_Agency) agreed to sponsor the EU countries, as well as Norway in a cooperative agreement to provide one million 155 mm artillery shells to Ukraine.[^cite_note-1millionShells-288][^cite_note-euPledges155mm-294]

On 20 March 2023, the Pentagon announced its 34th drawdown for Ukraine since August 2021, valued at up to \$350 million, including HIMARS rockets, 155 mm artillery shells, and 25 mm rounds.[^cite_note-34thPresDrawdown-295]

On 4 April 2023, the Pentagon announced up to \$500 million in aid to Ukraine: Additional munitions for Patriot air defense systems;

- Additional ammunition for High Mobility Artillery Rocket Systems;

- Artillery rounds: 155 mm and 105 mm.

- Mortar rounds: 120 mm.

- Tank ammunition: 120 mm and 105 mm.

- Ammunition: 25 mm.

- Tube-launched, optically tracked, wire-guided missiles.

- About 400 grenade launchers and 200,000 rounds of ammunition.

- Tactical vehicles to recover equipment: 11.

- Heavy fuel tankers: 61.

- Heavy equipment transport: 10 trucks and 10 trailers.

- Testing and diagnostic equipment to support vehicle maintenance and repair.

- Spare parts and other field equipment.

  [^cite_note-4apr2023-296]

In addition, \$2.1 billion in USAI for Ukraine included:

- Additional munitions for National Advanced Surface-to-Air Missile Systems.

- Nine counter-unmanned serial system 30 mm gun trucks.

- Mobile counter-unmanned aircraft system, or C-UAS, laser-guided rocket systems: 10.

- Three air surveillance radars.

- Anti-aircraft ammunition: 30 mm and 23 mm.

- Artillery rounds: 130 mm and 122 mm.

- Grad rockets: 122 mm.

- Rocket launchers and ammunition.

- Mortar systems: 120 mm and 81 mm.

- Mortar rounds: 120 mm, 81 mm, and 60 mm.

- Tank ammunition: 120 mm.

- Javelin anti-armor systems.

- Anti-armor rockets.

- Precision aerial munitions.

- About 3,600 small arms and more than 23,000,000 rounds of small-arms ammunition.

- Seven tactical vehicles to recover equipment.

- Eight heavy fuel tankers and 105 fuel trailers.

- Armored bridging systems.

- Four logistics support vehicles.

- Trucks and 10 trailers to transport heavy equipment.

- Secure communications equipment.

- Satellite communications terminals and services, and

- Funding for training, maintenance and sustainment.

  [^cite_note-4apr2023-296]

By 19 April 2023, another \$325 million in aid was heading to Ukraine, including additional ammunition for high-mobility artillery rocket systems; 155 mm and 105 mm artillery rounds; tube-launched, optically tracked wire-guided missiles for the U.S.-provided Bradley armored fighting vehicles; AT-4 anti-armor weapon systems; anti-tank mines; demolition munitions for obstacle clearing; over 9 million rounds of small arms ammunition; four logistics support vehicles; and precision aerial munitions,[^cite_note-19apr2023-276]

On 3 May 2023, US Secretary of State Antony Blinken announced the 37th drawdown package for Ukraine of up to \$300 million in materiel.[^cite_note-37thPresDraw-297] The package had been reported by Associated Press.[^cite_note-37thAidPackage-298][^cite_note-easternHerald37-299] The package included:

- Additional ammunition for High Mobility Artillery Rocket Systems (HIMARS);

- 155 mm howitzers;

- 155 mm artillery rounds;

- 120 mm, 81 mm, and 60 mm mortar rounds;

- Tube-Launched, Optically-Tracked, Wire-Guided (TOW) missiles;

- AT-4 and Carl Gustaf anti-armor weapon systems;

- Hydra-70 aircraft rockets;

- Small arms and small arms ammunition;

- Demolition munitions for obstacle clearing;

- Trucks and trailers to transport heavy equipment;

- Testing and diagnostic equipment to support vehicle maintenance and repair; and

- Spare parts and other field equipment.

  [^cite_note-blinken37th-300]

  [^cite_note-37thAidPackage-298]

  &#x20;An air-to-ground munition, the [Hydra 70](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydra_70) 70 mm air-launched rocket was donated for the first time.

  [^cite_note-reuters2May23-301]

On 9 May 2023, the Pentagon announced a new [USAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Security_Assistance_Initiative) package for Ukraine; under USAI, the materiel will not arrive in Ukraine as quickly as that of a Presidential drawdown from DoD stocks; instead, up to \$1.2 billion in contracts between DoD (US Department of Defense) and its defense vendors are being arranged for future delivery to Ukraine.[^cite_note-9may23-302] This support is intended to bolster Ukraine's air defense,[^cite_note-9may23-302] as well as to augment the artillery stocks.[^cite_note-howitzerProduction-316]

On 11 May 2023, the UK announced its contribution of [Storm Shadow](306908.md) cruise missiles.[^cite_note-stormShadow-317][^cite_note-stormShadow2LogisticsImpact-318]

&#x20;On 14 May 2023, Germany announced the contribution of €2.7 billion (\$2.95 billion, which doubles its contribution since the beginning of the invasion of 24 February 2022) in aid to Ukraine, including 30 Leopard 1A5 main battle tanks, 20 armored infantry fighting vehicles, four IRIS-T SLM air defense systems, 100 armored fighting vehicles, and 200 drones.[^cite_note-SchuetzeKimBengali-319]

On 31 May 2023, the US announced the 39th presidential drawdown package, worth up to \$300 million.[^cite_note-39thDrawdown-320] The aid consists of additional missiles for the Patriot air defense systems, Avenger air defense systems, Stinger antiaircraft systems and AIM-7 missiles for air defense.[^cite_note-39thDrawdown-320] In addition more artillery and antiarmor capabilities, and precision aerial munitions was provided.[^cite_note-39thDrawdown-320]

On 1 June 2023, [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) announced that the [Department of Defense](7279897.md) will buy [Starlink satellite services for Ukraine's communications network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starlink_satellite_services_in_Ukraine).[^cite_note-\:0-321] SpaceX had until then been providing Ukrainians and the country's military with Starlink internet service through private donations and under a separate contract with a U.S. foreign aid agency.[^cite_note-\:0-321]

On 9 June 2023, the US donated up to \$2.1 billion in [Ukraine Security Assistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Security_Assistance_Initiative) (USAI):&#x20;

- Additional munitions for [Patriot air defense systems](52024.md);

- [HAWK air defense systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIM-23_Hawk) and missiles;

- 105 mm and 203 mm artillery rounds;

- Puma Unmanned Aerial Systems;

- Laser-guided rocket system munitions;

- Support for training, maintenance, and sustainment activities.

  [^cite_note-usai9jun2023-322]

  &#x20;Unlike the US drawdown packages, in which materiel arrives immediately, the USAI is meant to aid Ukraine over the course of the ongoing campaign over the upcoming months.

  [^cite_note-usai9jun2023-322]

On 13 June 2023, the US donated its 40th drawdown package to Ukraine since August 2021:[^cite_note-40thDrawdownPackage-323]

- Additional munitions for National Advanced Surface-to-Air Missile Systems ([NASAMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASAMS));

- [Stinger anti-aircraft systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIM-92_Stinger);

- Additional ammunition for High Mobility Artillery Rocket Systems ([HIMARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIMARS));

- 155 mm and 105 mm artillery rounds;

- 15 [Bradley Infantry Fighting Vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_Infantry_Fighting_Vehicle);

- 10 [Stryker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stryker) Armored Personnel Carriers;

- [Javelin anti-armor systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FGM-148_Javelin);

- [Tube-Launched, Optically-Tracked, Wire-Guided (TOW)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BGM-71_TOW) missiles;

- [AT-4 anti-armor systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT4);

- Over 22 million rounds of small arms ammunition and grenades;

- [Demolition munitions for obstacle clearing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M58_MICLIC);

  [^cite_note-deminingExampleLevadnePryiutne-325]

- Tactical secure communications support equipment; and

- Spare parts and other field equipment.

  [^cite_note-40thDrawdownPackage-323]

The package is worth up to \$325 million;[^cite_note-40thDrawdownPackage-323] since the beginning of the invasion, the US has committed over \$39 billion in aid to Ukraine.[^cite_note-39billion-326]

The aid was announced on the eve of the 13th [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group) meeting in [Brussels](3708.md).[^cite_note-39billion-326][^cite_note-austinAbramsAssessmt-328][^cite_note-acctgErrFY22FY23-18]

On 27 June 2023, the US donated its 41st package to Ukraine since August 2021, a [USAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Security_Assistance_Initiative) donation.[^cite_note-41stPackageIsUSAI-330] The package is worth up to \$500 million including additional armored vehicles (25 Strykers and 30 Bradleys),[^cite_note-cbs27June2023-331] more artillery rounds, Javelins, Stingers,[^cite_note-cbs27June2023-331] and critical munitions for U.S.-provided [Patriot air defense systems](52024.md) and high-mobility artillery rocket systems ([HIMARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIMARS)). Total aid from the US to Ukraine has amounted to more than \$40.5 billion as of 27 June 2023.[^cite_note-acctgErrFY22FY23-18]

On 7 July 2023, the US provided [Patriot air defense systems](52024.md)[^cite_note-patriotsCanCounterHypersonics-333] and high-mobility artillery rocket systems ([HIMARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIMARS)), as well as hundreds of thousands of rounds of artillery; this is the 42nd drawdown for Ukraine, up to \$800 million.[^cite_note-dpicm-334] In addition, the US might provide [DPICM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DPICM) (cluster antipersonnel) shells.[^cite_note-dpicm-334][^cite_note-ukObjection-335][^cite_note-esObjection-336]

On 25 July 2023 the 43rd drawdown from US stocks gave Ukraine up to \$400 million in military aid, including [HIMARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIMARS) and [NASAMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NASAMS) rockets, [Hydra 70](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydra_70) rockets, [Stingers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIM-92_Stinger) and [Javelins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FGM-148_Javelin), 155 mm artillery shells, 32 [Stryker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stryker) armoured combat vehicles, mine clearance equipment, mortars and millions of small-arms rounds, and miniature [Hornet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Hornet_Nano) drones.[^cite_note-43rdDrawdown-17]

On 14 August 2023 the 44th drawdown from US stocks donated materiel worth up to \$200 million to Ukraine, including rockets for the [MIM-104 Patriot](52024.md) air defense systems and high-mobility artillery rocket systems ([HIMARS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HIMARS)).[^cite_note-44thDrawdown-337]

On 29 August 2023 the 45th Presidential drawdown from US stocks donated up to \$250 million to Ukraine, including:

- AIM-9M missiles for air defense;

- Additional ammunition for High Mobility Artillery Rocket Systems, also called HIMARS;

- 155 mm and 105 mm artillery rounds;

- Mine-clearing equipment;

- Tube-launched, optically tracked, wire-guided, or "TOW" missiles;

- Javelin and other anti-armor systems and rockets;

- Hydra-70 rockets;

- Over 3 million rounds of small-arms ammunition;

- Armored medical treatment vehicles and high-mobility multipurpose wheeled vehicle, or Humvee, ambulances;

- Demolitions munitions for obstacle clearing;

- Spare parts, maintenance, and other field equipment.

  [^cite_note-45thPda-338]

By August 2023 the US had supplied over 2 million 155 mm artillery rounds to Ukraine since 24 February 2022.[^cite_note-euProd155mmShells-339]

On 6 September 2023 the 46th Presidential drawdown from US stocks donated up to \$175 million to Ukraine, including additional air defense equipment; 155 mm and 105 mm artillery rounds; and other anti-armor systems and rockets.[^cite_note-46thDrawdown-340] The tranche is provisioned from the \$6.2 billion in presidential drawdown authority restored in June 2023, leaving \$5.75 billion in restored funding remaining.[^cite_note-46thDrawdown-340]

On 7 September 2023 the Biden administration announced a [USAI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USAI) package of up to \$600 billion for Ukraine:

- Equipment to sustain and integrate Ukraine's air defense systems;

- Additional ammunition for High Mobility Artillery Rocket Systems (HIMARS);

- 105 mm artillery rounds;

- Electronic warfare and counter-electronic warfare equipment;

- Demolition munitions for obstacle clearing;

- Mine clearing equipment; and

- Support and equipment for training, maintenance, and sustainment activities.

  [^cite_note-usai7Sep23-341]

On 21 September 2023 the 47th drawdown package for Ukraine, released after President Zelenskyy's visit to Washington DC, was valued at up to \$325 million.[^cite_note-47thPackage-342][^cite_note-21Sep2023FactSheet-251] That Thursday, 21 September 2023, President Biden informed President Zelenskyy that the US would be willing to provide Ukraine with [ATACMS](147015.md) missiles.[^cite_note-atacms-343][^cite_note-21Sep2023FactSheet-251] ATACMS missiles were secretly[^cite_note-detchSecretDelivery-344] delivered to Ukraine before 17 October 2023, when they were used to hit [Berdiansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berdiansk) airfield.[^cite_note-atacmsHitBerdiansk-345]

&#x20;On 11 October 2023 as announced during the 16th meeting of the Ukraine Defense Contact Group (UDCG), UDCG partner nation Germany has donated an additional \$1.1 billion package for Ukraine, including a Patriot system and additional Patriot missiles.[^cite_note-udcgOct23-346] US Secretary of Defense Lloyd Austin pointed out that Germany, the United Kingdom, and Poland, have each donated more by percentage of their GDP than has the US.[^cite_note-udcgOct23-346] By 3 November 2023 the US had announced its 50th security package for Ukraine, worth up to \$425 million.[^cite_note-50thSecurityPackage-347] By 17 November 2023 the goal of [§one million 155 mm artillery shells within one year](#17_March_2023) for Ukraine from European manufacturers was shown to be unrealistic,[^cite_note-ukr155MmEu-290] requiring more than the signing of contracts, but also the allocation of manufacturing resources, and the consideration of existing logistic contracts within the European defence community; the defence trade association had to remind the community that it takes time to include this important capability.[^cite_note-EuMiss155mm-348][^cite_note-euPledges155mm-294] By comparison, as of December 2022 the US Army looked to increasing 155 mm howitzer shell production to 20,000 rounds per month by spring 2024, and to 40,000 rounds per month by 2025.[^cite_note-usVsEu-349] On 23 January 2024 NATO signed contracts worth \$1.2 billion,[^cite_note-18thUdcg-350] for 220,000 155 mm artillery rounds for resupply of the NATO alliance.[^cite_note-1YrLate-351] The resupply agreement will not provide shells for 24 to 36 months.[^cite_note-1YrLate-351][^cite_note-euPledges155mm-294]

While on a visit to Kyiv, US Secretary of Defense Lloyd Austin announced a \$100 million aid package to Ukraine (the 51st), a Presidential drawdown from US stocks consisting of 155 mm artillery shells, additional interceptors for air defence, and antitank weapons.[^cite_note-2023KyivVisit-352][^cite_note-aidAsOfNov23-353] Germany has pledged \$1.4 billion in aid to Ukraine, including 4 IRIS-T air defense systems, 20,000 more 155 mm artillery shells, and antitank mines.[^cite_note-GermanPledge21Nov23-354] The 155 mm artillery shells are in addition to 140,000 155 mm rounds which Germany pledged to deliver in 2024, an \$8.5 billion pledge.[^cite_note-GermanPledge21Nov23-354][^cite_note-euPledges155mm-294] On 22 November 2023 US Secretary of Defense Lloyd Austin opened the 17th meeting of the [Ukraine Defense Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_Defense_Contact_Group) in a virtual session online; the \$100 million US package (the 51st) which was announced 20 November additionally included a HIMARS system.[^cite_note-glsdbImminent-121][^cite_note-17thUdcg-355]

On 4 November 2023 the Swedish Ministry of Defense confirmed its delivery of 8 [Archer Artillery Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archer_Artillery_System) to Ukraine.[^cite_note-8Archer155mm-356] An Archer howitzer is self-propelled, is capable of firing 9 shells in one minute,[^cite_note-archerCapability-357] and can replenish its 155 mm shells within 10 minutes, when resupplied by its munitions carrier. Sweden's contribution to Ukraine has amounted to \$2 billion as of November 2023.[^cite_note-8Archer155mm-356]

On 12 December 2023 the US Department of Defense announced its 53rd package for Ukraine, a \$200 million package, including AIM-9M missiles for air defense; additional ammunition for HIMARS; 155 mm and 105 mm artillery rounds; High-speed Anti-radiation missiles (HARMs); Tube-Launched, Optically-Tracked, Wire-Guided (TOW) missiles; Javelin and AT-4 anti-armor systems; Demolitions munitions for obstacle clearing.[^cite_note-53rdPkg-358]

On 27 December 2023 the US Department of Defense announced its 54th package for Ukraine since August 2021, a \$250 million package.[^cite_note-54thPkg-359] The aid included munitions for air defense munitions, as well as air defense system components. Additional ammunition was for high mobility artillery rocket systems, as well as for 155 mm and 105 mm artillery, for anti-armor munitions, and for over 15 million rounds of small-arms ammunition for Ukraine.[^cite_note-last23Pkg-360]

On 23 January 2024 the 18th meeting of the Ukraine Defense Contact Group (UDCG) was held virtually.[^cite_note-18thUdcg-350] Two coalitions were formed to benefit Ukraine, one for drones led by Latvia, and one for armor led by Poland.[^cite_note-2coalitions-361] Ground-Launched Small Diameter Bombs ([§ GLSDBs](#GLSDB))— which are compatible with HIMARS and GMLRS— were tested 16 January 2024 in preparation for their deployment to Ukraine.[^cite_note-deployGlsdb-362][^cite_note-250pounderM26-282]

On 14 February 2024 the 19th meeting of the Ukraine Defense Contact Group (UDCG) was held virtually. DoD Secretary Austin highlighted six UDCG coalitions:[^cite_note-19thUdcg-363]

1. Led by the US, Denmark, and the Netherlands: air-force capabilities;

   [^cite_note-19thUdcg-363]

2. Led by the US, and France: coalition on artillery;

   [^cite_note-19thUdcg-363]

3. Led by Germany, and France: coalition on ground-based air defense;

   [^cite_note-capabilityCoalitions-364]

4. Led by the UK, and Norway: coalition on maritime security;

   [^cite_note-maritimeCapability-365]

5. Led by Poland, a coalition on armor:

   [^cite_note-2coalitions-361]

   &#x20;and Estonia: coalition on IT;

   [^cite_note-capabilityCoalitions-364]

6. Led by Luxembourg, and Latvia: a coalition on drones;

   [^cite_note-2coalitions-361]

   &#x20;and a coalition on demining led by Lithuania.

   [^cite_note-deminingCoalition-366]

At the Munich Security conference on 18 February 2024 the Czech Republic announced it had found suppliers of at least 800,000 155 mm artillery shells located in non-Western countries for \$1.5 billion. The shells would have to be supplied to Ukraine via a middleman as the suppliers are not willing to supply Ukraine directly. A coalition of Belgium, Canada, Denmark, The Netherlands and about one dozen other countries could fund 400,000 of those 155 mm shells, as of 1 March 2024.[^cite_note-politForbesAxe-367]

On 12 March 2024 US National Security Advisor Jake Sullivan announced that a \$300 million cost savings in 2024 procurement has enabled the US to ship a security assistance package to Ukraine, consisting of "Stinger anti-aircraft missiles, HIMARS rockets, 155 mm high-explosive and cluster-munition artillery rounds, 105 mm artillery rounds, 84 anti-armor systems, and small arms ammunition".[^cite_note-costSavings300M-368] In April 2024 more [ATACMS](147015.md) missiles were secretly dilivered to Ukraine.[^cite_note-400MillPdPkg-369]

In early April 2024, the Prime Minister of [Latvia](17514.md), Evika Silina, made an announcement regarding Latvia's plan to send drones worth one million euros to [Ukraine](31750.md) soon. She mentioned that as a member of the Drone Coalition, Latvia intends to allocate a minimum of EUR 10 million in 2024 for the acquisition of drones.[^cite_note-\:1-25]

### Fighter jets for Ukraine

&#x20;Poland announced its [MiG-29](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MiG-29) fighter jets would be transferred to Ukraine, although one will be retained for a military museum in Poland.[^cite_note-mig29sFromPoland-370] Four MiGs were immediately transferred, the remainder were refurbished before their transfer,[^cite_note-4migsNow-371] On 17 March 2023, Slovakia agreed to give Ukraine its MiG-29 fighter jets.[^cite_note-13moreMigs-372] All 13 aircraft arrived by 17 April 2023.[^cite_note-all13Delivered-373]

On 20 January 2023, the Netherlands proposed donating US-made [F-16](11642.md) fighter planes to Ukraine;[^cite_note-f16sLeopard2s-210] this would require US permission.[^cite_note-UsPermitsF16s-374] By 19 May, the coalition agreed to train Ukrainian pilots on F-16 simulators in Europe.[^cite_note-UsPermitsF16s-374] This would allow the Netherlands, Belgium, Denmark, and Norway to donate F-16s to Ukraine, eventually.[^cite_note-UsPermitsF16s-374] At the G-7 summit in Hiroshima Japan on 20 May 2023, US president Biden announced that the United States would also train Ukrainian pilots to fly F-16s.[^cite_note-UsWillTrainF16Pilots-375] Poland, Denmark, the Netherlands, Belgium, Norway, and the US are in the planning stage for the F-16 training.[^cite_note-f16TrainingPlans23-376]

Ten F-16s cost \$1 billion and their sustainment cost another \$1 billion.[^cite_note-12thUdcg25May2023-377] By 25 May 2023, the nations of the UDCG had donated \$65 billion to Ukraine's defense.[^cite_note-12thUdcg25May2023-377][^cite_note-ukrAidTotals-378] The US approved F-16 training for Ukrainian pilots and maintenance crews.[^cite_note-usApprovesF16trainingForUkraine-379][^cite_note-pilotsInSimulators-380] The training took three to four months.[^cite_note-chrisCappy-381] As of 18 August, the Netherlands and Denmark had confirmed they had US approval to transfer F-16s to Ukraine.[^cite_note-danishDutchF16s-382] Ukraine could start to receive dozens of F-16s in December 2023 and January 2024.[^cite_note-chrisCappy-381] As of 27 December 2023, "at least 14 Ukrainian Air Force pilots now have some training on the" [F-16](11642.md).[^cite_note-14pilots-383] Six F-16s from Denmark are now expected in Ukraine in the second quarter of 2024, out of Denmark's total pledge of 19 aircraft by 2025.[^cite_note-f16s2Q2024-384][^cite_note-ryanPickrell-385][^cite_note-r47476-386] F-16s will start operating in Ukraine after 5 May 2024.[^cite_note-f16sAfter5May-387]

### European purchases of Indian artillery ammunition

On 19 September 2024, Reuters revealed European countries have been purchasing artillery shells from Indian “arms makers”, then transferring these shells to Ukraine. The Indian government hasn't officially authorised such exports. The Kremlin raised the issue with New Delhi on at least two occasions.[^cite_note-388]

## Support for Russia

Main article: [Support for Russia in the Russo-Ukrainian war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Support_for_Russia_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war)

Support for Russia since the start of the war has come from countries such as Belarus, North Korea, China, and Iran. This support has been principally military in nature.[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-sky8mar23-390][^cite_note-391][^cite_note-dualUse-392]

## Foreign military involvement

### Ukraine

Ukraine has actively sought volunteers from other countries after the beginning of the Russian invasion. On March 1, 2022, Ukraine temporarily lifted visa requirements for foreign volunteers who wished to join the fight against Russian forces. The move came after Zelenskyy created the [International Legion of Territorial Defense of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Legion_\(Ukraine\)) and called on volunteers to "join the defense of Ukraine, Europe and the world".[^cite_note-393] The US also assisted Ukraine with military planning, including war-gaming counter-offensive options.[^cite_note-economist-20221215-394]

Ukraine's foreign minister [Dmytro Kuleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Kuleba) stated as of 6 March 2022, approximately 20,000 foreign nationals from 52 countries had volunteered to fight.[^cite_note-Abend-2022-03-07-395] Most of these volunteers joined the newly created International Legion of Territorial Defense of Ukraine.[^cite_note-Abend-2022-03-07-395] On June 9, the [Donetsk People's Republic](42425747.md) sentenced three foreign volunteers to death; two of them were British citizens and one was a Moroccan national.[^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397] The prisoners were later released.[^cite_note-398]

NATO, US and the EU have publicly taken a strict policy of "no boots on the ground" in Ukraine.[^cite_note-399] However, since the start of the invasion, the United States has increased the covert involvement of its special operations forces and [CIA](5183633.md) officers in support of Ukraine.[^cite_note-ciaFeb2022-180] In April 2023, [classified documents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Pentagon_document_leaks) on Western special operations forces involvement in the war were leaked online. According to the document, dated 23 March 2023, the UK had the largest contingent of special operations personnel in Ukraine (50), followed by fellow NATO states Latvia (17), France (15), the US (14), and the Netherlands (1).[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] The US personnel were posted to the US embassy in Kyiv to provide security for VIPs, and to assist with oversight of US equipment and supplies being sent to Ukraine.[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404]

On 8 November 2024, US President [Joe Biden](145422.md) allowed American private military contractors to operate in Ukraine.[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] Per the [United States Department of Defense](7279897.md), these contractors will help Ukraine repair and maintain military equipment.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414][^cite_note-415]

### Russia

On 3 March 2022, [Russian Defense Ministry](7875884.md) spokesman [Igor Konashenkov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Konashenkov) warned [mercenaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unlawful_combatant) are not entitled to protection under the [Geneva Conventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Conventions) and that captured foreign fighters would not be considered prisoners of war but prosecuted as criminals.[^cite_note-416] On March 11, Moscow announced 16,000 volunteers from the Middle East were ready to join other pro-Russian foreign fighters alongside the Donbas separatists.[^cite_note-417] A video uploaded online showed armed Central African paramilitaries preparing to fight with Russian troops in Ukraine.[^cite_note-418]

On 8 April 2025, two Chinese nationals fighting for Russia were captured by Ukrainian forces, with intelligence suggesting more Chinese citizens could be fighting on behalf of Russia.[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420]

#### Iran

Main article: [Iran and the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_and_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\))

On 21 October 2022, a White House press release stated Iranian troops were in Crimea assisting Russia to launch [drone attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russian_strikes_against_Ukrainian_infrastructure) against civilians and civilian infrastructure.[^cite_note-421] On November 24, Ukrainian officials said the military had killed ten Iranians and would target any further Iranian military presence in Ukraine.[^cite_note-422]

#### North Korea

Main article: [North Korean involvement in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)](78396607.md)

North Korea has publicately denied any support for Russia and its war against Ukraine, but on 3 October 2024, Ukraine made a missile strike in Russian-occupied Donetsk Oblast, claiming that over 20 were killed, including six North Korean officers with three more North Korean servicemen wounded.[^cite_note-423] Later from 8 October, Ukraine and South Korea both claimed that North Koreans troops had been deployed to the battlefield to help with the launch missiles, and had suffered several casualties.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425][^cite_note-426] A week later, a White House spokesperson said that the United States was "concerned" about reports of North Korean soldiers fighting for Russia in Ukraine.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428][^cite_note-429]

On 18 October, the South Korean [National Intelligence Service](991544.md) said that North Korea was sending troops to fight with Russia in Ukraine. [South Korean President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Korean_President) [Yoon Suk Yeol](62877952.md) called for a security meeting and called for the international community to respond with "all available means".[^cite_note-430] A few days later, on 23 October, United States defence secretary [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) said that there was evidence that [North Korean troops](21263.md) were in [Russia](25391.md) and preparing to travel to the battlefield in [Ukraine](31750.md).[^cite_note-431] By 25 October Ukraine reported that the first North Korean military units had entered the active "warzones" in [Russia](25391.md)'s [Kursk Oblast](480916.md). The North Korean soldiers were reportedly under the command of Deputy Defence Minister [Yunus-bek Yevkurov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yunus-bek_Yevkurov).[^cite_note-432] A Lithuanian NPO reported that the first North Korean unit that entered in warzone was almost entirely killed, with only 1 survivor.[^cite_note-433] On 28 October [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) said that the U.S. will not impose new limits on Ukraine's use of American weapons if North Korea joins Russia's war, as NATO said North Korean military units had been deployed to the Kursk region in Russia.[^cite_note-434] The US secretary of state, [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken), said on 31 October that about 8,000 North Korean soldiers were stationed in Russia on the border with Ukraine, warning that Moscow is preparing to deploy those troops into combat “in the coming days”.[^cite_note-435]

On 4 November 2024, Ukrainian officials claimed military engagement with North Korean soldiers deployed in the Kursk region, marking the first instance of foreign military intervention since Russia's 2022 full-scale invasion.[^cite_note-436] On 21 November 2024, anonymous western officials reported that a Ukraine missile strike on a Russian army command and communication post in the Kursk region the day before had wounded a senior North Korean general.[^cite_note-437]

On 18 November 2024, the [Khyzhak Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khyzhak_Brigade_\(Ukraine\)) claimed that it had killed two "North Korean mercenaries" in Kursk Oblast,[^cite_note-438] and during an off-camera, on-the-record press briefing, Pentagon spokesman Major General [Pat Ryder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Ryder) told reporters on 16 December 2024 that North Korean soldiers had suffered casualties in the Kursk region.[^cite_note-439] He also said that there were 12,000 North Korean soldiers in Russia.[^cite_note-440] On 14 December 2024 the Ukrainian military intelligence agency ([HUR](1406269.md)) reported that 8 Russian soldiers of the Chechen [Akhmat unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akhmat_battalions) had been killed after their vehicles had been fired on by North Koreans in a friendly fire incident caused by the language barrier between them.[^cite_note-441] On 26 December 2024, the Ukrainian military news website [Militarnyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Militarnyi) reported that a North Korean soldier had been captured by Ukrainian special forces during an operation in Kursk Oblast. A picture of him was published in a Telegram Channel and he was described as appearing to be in a serious condition with wounds visible.[^cite_note-442] On 27 December 2024, the South Korean [National Intelligence Service](991544.md) claimed the first capture of a North Korean [soldier](21263.md) fighting against [Ukrainian forces](7433327.md) in [Kursk Oblast](480916.md), [Russia](25391.md), with the soldier later dying from his injuries. Zelenskyy said on the same day that several badly wounded North Korean soldiers have died after being captured by Ukraine during the war as they were to injured to be saved.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445] In an interview with the [Korean Broadcasting System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_Broadcasting_System), the Ukrainian Defense Minister, [Rustem Umierov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rustem_Umierov) claimed that North Koreans were "difficult to identify" due to similarities to [Buryats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buryats).[^cite_note-auto-446] He also claimed that North Koreans soldiers were wearing Russian uniforms which further complicated identification.[^cite_note-auto-446] On 11 January 2025, Ukraine claimed to have captured alive two other North Korean soldiers.[^cite_note-447] On 13 December 2025, [Kim Jong Un](5962511.md) announced that 9 North Korean troops working as part a mine clearing regiment in Kursk had been killed since the beginning of the 120 day deployment in August.[^cite_note-448]

On 26 April 2026, North Korea publicly opened the Memorial Museum of Foreign Military Operations in a ceremony attended by Russian Defence Minister [Andrei Belousov](36245360.md) and [Chairman of the State Duma](5157139.md) [Vyacheslav Volodin](32671558.md).[^cite_note-449] The museum commemorates the official participation of North Korean soldiers who fought and died in Kursk. Captured equipment were displayed like the American [M1A1 Abrams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M1_Abrams), German [Leopard 2A4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leopard_2), German [Marder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marder_\(infantry_fighting_vehicle\)) IFV, French [AMX-10 RC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMX-10_RC), and Turkish [Kirpi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMC_Kirpi) MRAP.[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451]

## International reactions



Protest of [Russians](59510.md) living in [Czech Republic](5321.md) against the war in Ukraine, on 26 March 2022

Main articles: [Government and intergovernmental reactions to the Russian invasion of Ukraine](70158804.md), [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2623](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2623), [United Nations General Assembly Resolution ES-11/1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/1), and [Legality of the 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](70272922.md)

The Russian invasion of Ukraine received [widespread international condemnation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and [protests occurred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) around the world. On March 2, the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) passed [UNGA resolution ES-11/1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_ES-11/1) condemning the invasion and demanding a full withdrawal of Russian forces.[^cite_note-auto3-452] The [International Court of Justice](14918.md) ordered Russia to suspend military operations and the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe) expelled Russia. Many countries [imposed sanctions](70323386.md) on Russia, which have [affected the economies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_impact_of_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) of Russia and the world;[^cite_note-Sanctions_slams_Russian_economy-453] and provided [humanitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_humanitarian_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) and [military aid to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War).[^cite_note-Al-Jazeera-2022-02-28-27] The [International Criminal Court](14880.md) opened [an investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Ukraine) into [crimes against humanity](61099.md) in Ukraine since 2013, as well as [war crimes during the 2022 invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-auto5-454]

Chinese and Belarusian troops began joint military exercises near Brest, close to the Polish border, on 8 July 2024. The "Eagle Assault" maneuvers, lasting until 19 July 2024, focused on anti-terrorist operations including night landings, overcoming water obstacles, and urban combat.[^cite_note-455]

The United States and Britain pledged around \$1.5 billion in additional aid to Ukraine on 11 September 2024. The U.S. declared over \$700 million in humanitarian aid, while Britain stated \$782 million in further aid and loan guarantees.[^cite_note-456]

The US's recent votes at the UN, aligning with Russia on key resolutions regarding the Russia-Ukraine conflict, underscore a shift in US policy. This has strained relations with European allies, who advocated for stronger actions against Russia, highlighting growing geopolitical divisions.[^cite_note-457]

### Economic sanctions against Russia

Main articles: [International sanctions during the Russian invasion of Ukraine](70323386.md), [Economic consequences of the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)](70188989.md), and [List of people and organizations sanctioned during the Russo-Ukrainian war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_and_organizations_sanctioned_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war)

Further information: [2020s energy crisis](68934947.md), [2022–2023 global food crises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_global_food_crises), [2022–2023 Russia–European Union gas dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_Russia%E2%80%93European_Union_gas_dispute), and [Restrictions on transit to Kaliningrad Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Restrictions_on_transit_to_Kaliningrad_Oblast)

See also: [Russia in the European energy sector](24648652.md) and [Economy of Russia](25705.md)

US President [Joe Biden](145422.md)'s statements and a short question and answer session on 24 February 2022

[Western](21208200.md) and other countries imposed limited sanctions on Russia when it recognized Donbas as an independent nation. When Russia invaded Ukraine in February 2022, many other countries applied sanctions intended to devastate the Russian economy.[^cite_note-458] The sanctions targeted individuals, banks, businesses, monetary exchanges, bank transfers, exports, and imports.[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] The sanctions cut off major Russian banks from [SWIFT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SWIFT), the global messaging network for international payments, but left some limited access to ensure the continued ability to pay for gas shipments.[^cite_note-461] Sanctions also included [asset freezes](5847461.md) on the [Russian Central Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Central_Bank), which holds \$630 billion in [foreign-exchange reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_foreign-exchange_reserves),[^cite_note-462] to prevent it from offsetting the effects of sanctions,[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464] and froze construction of the [Nord Stream 2](50335419.md) gas pipeline.[^cite_note-465] By 1 March 2022, \$1 trillion of Russian assets were frozen by sanctions.[^cite_note-466]



[EUR](9472.md)/[Ruble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruble) exchange rate (Rubles per Euro)



&#x20; Russian inflation rate

&#x20; [Central Bank of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Russia) key interest rate



Russian bonds, [inverted yield curves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inverted_yield_curve) to tame inflation during their wars ([Russo-Georgian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russo-Georgian_War), [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md), 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine)&#x20;

&#x20; 20 year bond

&#x20; 10 year bond

&#x20; 1 year bond

&#x20; 3 month bond

[Kristalina Georgieva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kristalina_Georgieva), managing director of the [International Monetary Fund](15251.md) (IMF), said the conflict posed a substantial economic risk, both regionally and internationally, and that the IMF could help other affected countries, in addition to the \$2.2 billion loan package for Ukraine. [David Malpass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Malpass), president of the [World Bank Group](36752.md), warned of far-reaching economic and social effects, and reported the bank was preparing significant economic and fiscal support to Ukraine and the region.[^cite_note-467]

Economic sanctions affected Russia from the first day of the invasion; its [stock market](52328.md) fell by up to 39% ([RTS Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTS_Index)). The [Russian ruble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_ruble) fell to record lows, and Russians rushed to exchange currency.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470] Stock exchanges in Moscow and Saint Petersburg closed until at least 18 March,[^cite_note-471] the longest-such closure in Russia's history.[^cite_note-472] On 26 February, [S\&P Global Ratings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S\&P_Global_Ratings) downgraded the Russian government [credit rating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_credit_rating) to "junk", causing funds that require investment-grade bonds to dump Russian debt, making further borrowing very difficult for Russia.[^cite_note-473] On 11 April, S\&P Global placed Russia under "selective default" on its foreign debt for insisting on payments in rubles.[^cite_note-474] Dozens of Western corporations, including [Unilever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unilever), [McDonald's](2480627.md), [Coca-Cola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola_company), [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks), [Hermès](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herm%C3%A8s), [Chanel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chanel), and [Prada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prada) ceased trading in Russia.[^cite_note-475]

On 24 March, [Joe Biden](145422.md)'s administration issued an executive order that barred the sale of Russian gold reserves by US citizens; other G7 leaders took similar action.[^cite_note-476] Russia has used gold to protect its economy from the effects of the sanctions that were imposed following the 2014 annexation of Crimea.[^cite_note-477] In April 2022, Russia supplied 45% of EU's natural gas imports, earning \$900 million a day.[^cite_note-478] Russia is the world's largest exporter of natural gas,[^cite_note-479] grains, and fertilisers, and is among the world's largest suppliers of crude oil, coal, and steel and other metals,[^cite_note-480] including palladium, platinum, gold, cobalt, nickel, and aluminum.[^cite_note-481][^cite_note-482]



&#x20;  Russia\
&#x20;   Countries on Russia's [unfriendly countries list](70389552.md). Countries and territories on the list have imposed or joined sanctions against Russia.

[^cite_note-483]

In May 2022, the [European Commission](9974.md) (EC) proposed a ban on oil imports from Russia.[^cite_note-484] European policy-makers decided to replace Russian fossil fuel imports with other fossil fuels imports and European coal energy production.[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486] Because of Russia's status as "a key supplier" of materials used for "clean energy technologies", reactions to the war in Ukraine may also have a negative impact on efforts to reduce [greenhouse gas emissions](9528025.md).[^cite_note-487] Due to the sanctions imposed on Russia, the country intended to capitalize on alternative trade routes.[^cite_note-488]

In March 2022, a [gas dispute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russia%E2%80%93European_Union_gas_dispute) between Russia and the EU began.[^cite_note-489] On 14 June, Russia's [Gazprom](320489.md) announced it would be reduce gas flow via the [Nord Stream 1](3498125.md) pipeline due to what it called [Siemens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siemens)' failure to promptly return compressor units that had been sent to Canada for repair. Germany's energy regulator challenged the explanation.[^cite_note-490]

On 17 June, President Putin spoke to investors at [St. Petersburg International Economic Forum](26267797.md) about economic sanctions, saying "the economic blitzkrieg against Russia had no chance of succeeding from the very beginning", that the sanctions would hurt the countries imposing them more than they would hurt Russia, and called the sanctions "mad and thoughtless". He said to the investors: "Invest here. It's safer in your own house. Those who didn't want to listen to this have lost millions abroad."[^cite_note-491]

In January 2023, the US imposed sanctions on the [Wagner Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group), a private militia that until his death that August was owned by [Yevgeny Prigozhin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yevgeny_Prigozhin), a Russian oligarch with close ties to Vladimir Putin. The expanded sanctions also targeted related companies and individuals who were involved in the mercenary activities concerning Ukraine.[^cite_note-492] In February 2023, the US urged the United Arab Emirates (UAE) and Egypt to persuade the military leaders of Libya and Sudan to end their associations with the Wagner Group. In Libya, the mercenary firm was assisting the UAE-backed military commander [Khalifa Haftar](31227894.md). The UAE was the main funder of the Wagner Group in Libya.[^cite_note-AP-03-02-2023-493][^cite_note-494] The Wagner Group had been involved in several African and Middle Eastern nations like Sudan, Libya, Mali, Syria, and the Central African Republic, where it deployed thousands of operatives.[^cite_note-AP-03-02-2023-493]

In March 2023, [Oleg Deripaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleg_Deripaska) said Russia could run out of money by 2024;[^cite_note-illiquidBy2024-495] In 2022, Russia ran a \$34 billion budget deficit.[^cite_note-34billionDeficit-496] The [Russian National Wealth Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_National_Wealth_Fund) was funding the war at the rate of \$8 to \$9 billion per month, which could exhaust its liquid funds by the end of 2023.[^cite_note-23mayBeTurningPt-497] This would affect the retirement pension system of Russia.[^cite_note-23mayBeTurningPt-497][^cite_note-sovereigntyAndLaw-498] In March 2023, [CCP General Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Secretary_of_the_Chinese_Communist_Party) [Xi Jinping](2017814.md) and Vladimir Putin [met in Moscow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_visit_by_Xi_Jinping_to_Russia), and agreed to cooperate over a wide range of business, and economic issues, such as payment in [renminbi](66958.md) or rubles.[^cite_note-zhRuEconCoop-499]

### United States sanctions against China

This section is an excerpt from [United States sanctions against China § Russo–Ukrainian war](64972789.md#Russo%E2%80%93Ukrainian_war).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=United_States_sanctions_against_China\&action=edit)]

In April 2022, United States Secretary of Treasury [Janet Yellen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janet_Yellen) warned China that it could face consequences for not sanctioning Russia.[^cite_note-500] In June 2022, the United States Department of Commerce placed five Hong Kong companies on the [Bureau of Industry and Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Industry_and_Security)'s Entity List for providing support to Russia's military.[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-502] The U.S. Treasury Department separately sanctioned a Chinese and an Armenian vendor for maintaining trade relationship with a Russian arms procurement firm.[^cite_note-United_States_sanctions_against_China_\:0-503][^cite_note-United_States_sanctions_against_China_\:10-504] In September 2022, the Office of Foreign Assets Control sanctioned Sinno Electronics of [Shenzhen](83368.md) for supplying a Russian military procurement network.[^cite_note-United_States_sanctions_against_China_\:0-503][^cite_note-505]

In January 2023, the U.S. Treasury Department sanctioned [Spacety China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spacety), also known as Changsha Tianyi Space Science and Technology Research Institute Co. Ltd., for providing satellite imagery to the [Wagner Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group).[^cite_note-506] In February 2023, the U.S. Commerce Department added AOOK Technology Ltd, Beijing Ti-Tech Science and Technology Development Co, Beijing Yunze Technology Co, and China HEAD Aerospace Technology Co to the Entity List for aiding Russia's military.[^cite_note-507][^cite_note-508][^cite_note-509]

In March 2023, the U.S. Treasury Department sanctioned five Chinese companies for supplying equipment to the [Iran Aircraft Manufacturing Industries Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Aircraft_Manufacturing_Industries_Corporation), which manufactures [HESA Shahed 136](71747103.md) drones used by Russia against Ukraine.[^cite_note-510][^cite_note-511]

In July 2023, the U.S. Office of the [Director of National Intelligence](456082.md) published a report stating that the Chinese government is assisting Russia to evade sanctions and providing it with dual-use technology.[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-513]

In October 2023, the U.S. Department of Commerce added 42 Chinese companies to the Entity List for supplying Russia with microelectronics for missile and drone guidance systems.[^cite_note-514] In April 2024, the Department of Commerce sanctioned a Chinese company for supporting Russia's military through the procurement, development, and proliferation of Russian drones.[^cite_note-515] In May 2024, the U.S. sanctioned 20 companies in mainland China and Hong Kong for supplying Russia's military.[^cite_note-516] In October 2024, the U.S. sanctioned two companies, [Xiamen Limbach Aircraft Engine Co.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limbach_Flugmotoren) and Redlepus Vector Industry, involving the production of long-range attack drones for Russia, including the [Garpiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garpiya).[^cite_note-517]

In June 2025, a majority of [U.S. senators](70645797.md) supported [secondary sanctions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_sanctions) against Russia that would impose 500% [tariffs](78448911.md) on countries that buy [Russian oil](10450931.md), [natural gas](42827258.md), [uranium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Russia), and other exports. China is one of the main consumers of Russian energy.[^cite_note-518]

## Other reactions

In response to the Russian invasion of Ukraine, Estonia removed a remaining Soviet-era monument from a square in [Narva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narva).[^cite_note-519] After its removal, Estonia was subjected to "the most extensive cyberattack" since the [2007 cyberattacks on Estonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_cyberattacks_on_Estonia).[^cite_note-520]

[^cite_note-acctgErrFY22FY23-18]: &#x20;On Tuesday 20 June 2023, the Pentagon disclosed it had made an accounting error when disbursing materiel from its stocks: In fiscal year 2022 (FY22) ending 30 Sep 2022, and in FY23 ending 30 Sep 2023, it had overestimated the value of the stocks, by using the replacement cost, rather than the book value of the presidential drawdown stocks.<sup>[\[302\]](#cite_note-moreMaterielIn2022,2023-329)</sup> The error amounts to \$6.2 billion which will be applied going forward. In FY22 the error was \$2.6 billion, and in FY23 the error was \$3.6 billion; the net result is that *more* materiel will be delivered to Ukraine going forward, as the funds have already been allocated.<sup>[\[302\]](#cite_note-moreMaterielIn2022,2023-329)</sup>

[^cite_note-euTanks-174]: &#x20;The US Abrams' lightest tanks weigh in at 60 tons, some 15 tons heavier than the weight limits of European roads and bridges. The latest Abrams versions are 74 tons. European rivers are wider than the span of Joint Assault Bridges. Thus specialized bridging operations must be designed by military engineers, case-by-case. *[Rasputitsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rasputitsa)* will defeat even the indigenous European armour. Thus military campaigns in Europe are dependent on individual weather conditions.

[^cite_note-abct-178]: &#x20;In US terms, an [§Armored brigade combat team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigade_combat_team#Armored_brigade_combat_team) (ABCT). Note by US Army doctrine 45 armed [§ M113s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M113_armored_personnel_carrier), Humvees and supply trucks for refuel and re-armament would be included. An ABCT would consist of two armored battalions (at 58 tanks apiece), as well as an infantry battalion, and a reconnaissance squadron. This organization would support [§ combined arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_arms#Post_Cold_War_\(1992%E2%80%93present\)) maneuver.

[^cite_note-armoredDivReq-182]: &#x20;Compare to Ukraine's request for 300 tanks.<sup>[\[170\]](#cite_note-300tankReq-181)</sup>

[^cite_note-combinedArms-184]: &#x20;In [combined arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_arms) armour and infantry do not fight alone; rather armour, infantry, artillery, logistics, aviation, and reconnaissance operate in concert, each branch working to support and protect the other branches,<sup>[\[171\]](#cite_note-basicTactics-183)</sup> as a combined team of teams, by US Army doctrine.

[^cite_note-howitzerShells-196]: &#x20;By early September 2022 the US had given 126 [M777](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M777) howitzer cannons and over 800,000 rounds of 155 mm ammunition for them.<sup>[\[181\]](#cite_note-peck-194)</sup> By January 2023 the US had donated 250,000 more 155 mm shells to Ukraine. [^cite_note-euPledges155mm-294]: &#x20;The 17 March 2023 pledge of one million 155 mm artillery shells to Ukraine<sup>[\[266\]](#cite_note-edaPledge1millionShells-289)</sup> has three parts 1) €1 billion to EU bloc members for the shells they donate from their stock; 2) €1 billion to fund EU states and Norway for manufacture of 155 mm shells; and 3) direct funding of investment in factories that manufacture these munitions.<sup>[\[265\]](#cite_note-1millionShells-288)</sup> It is the 3rd provision that prioritises the allocation of critical materials to manufacturing processes, which is alarming some European manufacturers.<sup>[\[267\]](#cite_note-ukr155MmEu-290)</sup> In December 2023 [Rheinmetall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rheinmetall) received an order for tens of thousands of 155 mm artillery shells destined for Ukraine.<sup>[\[268\]](#cite_note-rheinmetallEsOrder-291)</sup> Shells from this order will be produced in Spain; the shells in this order begin delivery to Ukraine by 2025.<sup>[\[268\]](#cite_note-rheinmetallEsOrder-291)</sup> Earlier orders from October 2023 include 150,000 155 mm Rheinmetall shells destined for Germany; In 2024, Rheinmetall plans to significantly increase its production capacity in Spain, Germany, South Africa and Australia, for an annual production capacity of 700,000 155 mm shells.<sup>[\[268\]](#cite_note-rheinmetallEsOrder-291)</sup> As of 6 December 2023, six EU countries have ordered 60,000 shells for Ukraine under the EU manufacturing agreement.<sup>[\[269\]](#cite_note-60000shells-292)</sup> Globally, defense spending jumped in 2023 in response to the Russian invasion of Ukraine.<sup>[\[270\]](#cite_note-globDefSpend-293)</sup>

[^cite_note-howitzerProduction-316]: &#x20;For example, 155 mm howitzer shells are to be manufactured in much greater quantities in the coming months and years<sup>[\[279\]](#cite_note-scrantonDemo-303)</sup><sup>[\[280\]](#cite_note-8Jp60vga6SE-304)</sup><sup>[\[281\]](#cite_note-bigOrders-305)</sup><sup>[\[282\]](#cite_note-155mmShells-306)</sup><sup>[\[283\]](#cite_note-contestedLogis-307)</sup><sup>[\[284\]](#cite_note-155mmCapacity-308)</sup><sup>[\[285\]](#cite_note-dib-309)</sup><sup>[\[286\]](#cite_note-targetedList-310)</sup><sup>[\[287\]](#cite_note-munitionsDemand-311)</sup> to augment both Ukraine's and DoD's future stocks of ammunition; but contracts for GMLRS, HIMARS, improved Stingers, Javelins,<sup>[\[288\]](#cite_note-fgm148Stats-312)</sup> etc. are being let.<sup>[\[289\]](#cite_note-oib-313)</sup><sup>[\[290\]](#cite_note-surges-314)</sup><sup>[\[291\]](#cite_note-laplante-315)</sup>

Soldiers [require MOSs— TCO\:14A; TCA\:14E; Other MOSs 14H; 14T; Tactical operations 140A, 140K](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_Army_careers#Air_Defense_Artillery_Branch_\(ADA\)). PAC-2 missiles use proximity fuses. PAC-3 missiles are Hit to Kill. PAC3-MSE is newest generation interceptor.
